    out = []
    for rid in range(n):
        t += rng.expovariate(rate)
        out.append(Request(rid, round(t, 6), rng.randint(*prompt), rng.randint(*output)))
    return out


def run_server(model, hw, requests, schedule):
    pending = sorted(requests, key=lambda r: (r.arrival, r.rid))
    queue, running, finished, log = [], [], [], []
    now, i = 0.0, 0
    while i < len(pending) or queue or running:
        while i < len(pending) and pending[i].arrival <= now:
            queue.append(pending[i])
            i += 1
        plan = schedule(queue, running, now) if queue or running else []
        if not plan:
            if i < len(pending):
                now = max(now, pending[i].arrival)
                continue
            if queue or running:
                raise RuntimeError("requests are waiting but the scheduler planned nothing")
            break
        for req, n in plan:
            if req in queue:
                queue.remove(req)
                running.append(req)
        now += step_cost(model, hw, [(n, req.cached) for req, n in plan])["time"]
        for req, n in plan:
            req.cached += n
            if req.cached >= req.prompt_len:
                req.generated += 1
                req.token_times.append(now)
        log.append((now, len(plan), sum(n for _, n in plan), sum(r.cached for r in running)))
        for req in [r for r in running if r.done()]:
            running.remove(req)
            finished.append(req)
    return finished, log


def one_at_a_time(queue, running, now):
    if running:
        return [(running[0], 1)]
    if queue:
        return [(queue[0], queue[0].prompt_len)]
    return []


def continuous(max_batch, max_tokens):
    def schedule(queue, running, now):
        plan = [(req, 1) for req in running]
        tokens = len(plan)
        for req in queue:
            if len(plan) >= max_batch or tokens + req.prompt_len > max_tokens:
                break
            plan.append((req, req.prompt_len))
            tokens += req.prompt_len
        return plan

    return schedule


def static(batch_size):
    def schedule(queue, running, now):
        if running:
            return [(req, 1) for req in running]
        return [(req, req.prompt_len) for req in queue[:batch_size]]

    return schedule
--- hint
Like `continuous`, `static` defines a function inside itself and returns it. The two cases are "something is running" and "nothing is".
--- hint
When nothing is running, `queue[:batch_size]` is the next batch; plan each of its requests with its `prompt_len`.
--- check case | Three requests, batch size 2: the third waits for the whole first batch
(lambda log: [n for _, n, _, _ in log])(run_server(Model(7_000_000_000, 32, 4096, 8, 128), Accel(3e14, 8.64e11, 48e9), [Request(0, 0.0, 50, 2), Request(1, 0.0, 50, 4), Request(2, 0.0, 50, 2)], static(2))[1])
=> [2, 2, 1, 1, 1, 1]
?? Step 2 decodes both, and request 0 is done. Then only request 1 is running for two more steps, but request 2 may not join until it ends.
--- check case | A request arriving mid-batch waits for the batch to end
(lambda f: [(r.rid, round(r.token_times[0] - r.arrival, 6)) for r in sorted(f, key=lambda r: r.rid)])(run_server(Model(7_000_000_000, 32, 4096, 8, 128), Accel(3e14, 8.64e11, 48e9), [Request(0, 0.0, 100, 50), Request(1, 0.01, 100, 2)], static(4))[0])
=> [(0, 0.016219), (1, 0.817348)]
--- check test | Every request finishes, and no batch is bigger than batch_size
(lambda res: len(res[0]) == 100 and max(n for _, n, _, _ in res[1]) <= 8)(run_server(Model(7_000_000_000, 32, 4096, 8, 128), Accel(3e14, 8.64e11, 48e9), make_requests(9, 100, 8.0), static(8)))
--- check test | On the same workload, continuous batching has a far lower median TTFT
(lambda w: (lambda s, c: sorted(c)[50] * 5 < sorted(s)[50])(*[[r.token_times[0] - r.arrival for r in run_server(Model(7_000_000_000, 32, 4096, 8, 128), Accel(3e14, 8.64e11, 48e9), w(), sch)[0]] for sch in (static(16), continuous(16, 4096))]))(lambda: make_requests(9, 100, 8.0))

+++ practice | How much load meets the SLO?
--- task
Capacity planning: find the highest arrival rate a server can take while still serving most requests well. The starter has the cost model, the server, and the lesson's `continuous` and `serving_metrics`.

Write `capacity(model, hw, rates, n, seed, ttft_slo, tpot_slo, target)`. For each rate in `rates` (in the order given), run `make_requests(seed, n, rate)` through `run_server` with `continuous(32, 2048)`, and compute the fraction of the requests that meet both objectives (TTFT ≤ `ttft_slo`, and TPOT ≤ `tpot_slo` for requests with 2 or more output tokens). Return a pair `(best, fractions)`: `fractions` is a list of those fractions, each rounded to 3 decimal places, in the order of `rates`; `best` is the largest rate whose fraction is at least `target`, or `None` if none is.

Use a fresh workload for each rate (requests are changed by a run).
--- starter


import math
import random


class Model:
    """The shapes of a decoder-only transformer that set what serving it costs."""

    def __init__(self, n_params, n_layers, d_model, n_kv_heads, head_dim, bytes_per_param=2, kv_bytes=2):
        self.n_params = n_params
        self.n_layers = n_layers
        self.d_model = d_model
        self.n_kv_heads = n_kv_heads
        self.head_dim = head_dim
        self.bytes_per_param = bytes_per_param
        self.kv_bytes = kv_bytes


class Accel:
    """One accelerator: peak FLOP/s, memory bandwidth in bytes/s, memory size in bytes."""

    def __init__(self, peak_flops, mem_bw, mem_bytes):
        self.peak_flops = peak_flops
        self.mem_bw = mem_bw
        self.mem_bytes = mem_bytes


def kv_bytes_per_token(model):
    return 2 * model.n_layers * model.n_kv_heads * model.head_dim * model.kv_bytes


def step_cost(model, hw, work):
    if not work:
        return {"flops": 0, "bytes": 0, "time": 0.0, "bound": "idle"}
    tokens = sum(n for n, _ in work)
    keys = sum(n * c + n * (n + 1) // 2 for n, c in work)
    flops = 2 * model.n_params * tokens + 4 * model.n_layers * model.d_model * keys
    kv = kv_bytes_per_token(model)
    moved = model.n_params * model.bytes_per_param + kv * sum(c + n for n, c in work)
    t_compute = flops / hw.peak_flops
    t_memory = moved / hw.mem_bw
    bound = "compute" if t_compute > t_memory else "memory"
    return {"flops": flops, "bytes": moved, "time": max(t_compute, t_memory), "bound": bound}


class Request:
    def __init__(self, rid, arrival, prompt_len, output_len):
        self.rid = rid
        self.arrival = arrival
        self.prompt_len = prompt_len
        self.output_len = output_len
        self.cached = 0
        self.generated = 0
        self.token_times = []

    def done(self):
        return self.generated >= self.output_len


def make_requests(seed, n, rate, prompt=(64, 512), output=(16, 256)):
    rng = random.Random(seed)
    t = 0.0
    out = []
    for rid in range(n):
        t += rng.expovariate(rate)
        out.append(Request(rid, round(t, 6), rng.randint(*prompt), rng.randint(*output)))
    return out


def run_server(model, hw, requests, schedule):
    pending = sorted(requests, key=lambda r: (r.arrival, r.rid))
    queue, running, finished, log = [], [], [], []
    now, i = 0.0, 0
    while i < len(pending) or queue or running:
        while i < len(pending) and pending[i].arrival <= now:
            queue.append(pending[i])
            i += 1
        plan = schedule(queue, running, now) if queue or running else []
        if not plan:
            if i < len(pending):
                now = max(now, pending[i].arrival)
                continue
            if queue or running:
                raise RuntimeError("requests are waiting but the scheduler planned nothing")
            break
        for req, n in plan:
            if req in queue:
                queue.remove(req)
                running.append(req)
        now += step_cost(model, hw, [(n, req.cached) for req, n in plan])["time"]
        for req, n in plan:
            req.cached += n
            if req.cached >= req.prompt_len:
                req.generated += 1
                req.token_times.append(now)
        log.append((now, len(plan), sum(n for _, n in plan), sum(r.cached for r in running)))
        for req in [r for r in running if r.done()]:
            running.remove(req)
            finished.append(req)
    return finished, log


def one_at_a_time(queue, running, now):
    if running:
        return [(running[0], 1)]
    if queue:
        return [(queue[0], queue[0].prompt_len)]
    return []


def continuous(max_batch, max_tokens):
    def schedule(queue, running, now):
        plan = [(req, 1) for req in running]
        tokens = len(plan)
        for req in queue:
            if len(plan) >= max_batch or tokens + req.prompt_len > max_tokens:
                break
            plan.append((req, req.prompt_len))
            tokens += req.prompt_len
        return plan

    return schedule


def percentile(values, p):
    ordered = sorted(values)
    return ordered[max(0, math.ceil(p / 100 * len(ordered)) - 1)]


def serving_metrics(finished, ttft_slo, tpot_slo):
    ttft = [r.token_times[0] - r.arrival for r in finished]
    tpot = {r.rid: (r.token_times[-1] - r.token_times[0]) / (r.output_len - 1)
            for r in finished if r.output_len > 1}
    start = min(r.arrival for r in finished)
    end = max(r.token_times[-1] for r in finished)
    duration = end - start
    tokens = sum(r.output_len for r in finished)
    good = sum(1 for r, t in zip(finished, ttft) if t <= ttft_slo and tpot.get(r.rid, 0.0) <= tpot_slo)
    tpots = list(tpot.values())
    return {
        "ttft_p50": round(percentile(ttft, 50), 6),
        "ttft_p99": round(percentile(ttft, 99), 6),
        "tpot_p50": round(percentile(tpots, 50), 6) if tpots else None,
        "tpot_p99": round(percentile(tpots, 99), 6) if tpots else None,
        "throughput": round(tokens / duration, 6),
        "goodput": round(good / duration, 6),
    }


def capacity(model, hw, rates, n, seed, ttft_slo, tpot_slo, target):
    return (None, [])
--- solution


import math
import random


class Model:
    """The shapes of a decoder-only transformer that set what serving it costs."""

    def __init__(self, n_params, n_layers, d_model, n_kv_heads, head_dim, bytes_per_param=2, kv_bytes=2):
        self.n_params = n_params
        self.n_layers = n_layers
        self.d_model = d_model
        self.n_kv_heads = n_kv_heads
        self.head_dim = head_dim
        self.bytes_per_param = bytes_per_param
        self.kv_bytes = kv_bytes


class Accel:
    """One accelerator: peak FLOP/s, memory bandwidth in bytes/s, memory size in bytes."""

    def __init__(self, peak_flops, mem_bw, mem_bytes):
        self.peak_flops = peak_flops
        self.mem_bw = mem_bw
        self.mem_bytes = mem_bytes


def kv_bytes_per_token(model):
    return 2 * model.n_layers * model.n_kv_heads * model.head_dim * model.kv_bytes


def step_cost(model, hw, work):
    if not work:
        return {"flops": 0, "bytes": 0, "time": 0.0, "bound": "idle"}
    tokens = sum(n for n, _ in work)
    keys = sum(n * c + n * (n + 1) // 2 for n, c in work)
    flops = 2 * model.n_params * tokens + 4 * model.n_layers * model.d_model * keys
    kv = kv_bytes_per_token(model)
    moved = model.n_params * model.bytes_per_param + kv * sum(c + n for n, c in work)
    t_compute = flops / hw.peak_flops
    t_memory = moved / hw.mem_bw
    bound = "compute" if t_compute > t_memory else "memory"
    return {"flops": flops, "bytes": moved, "time": max(t_compute, t_memory), "bound": bound}


class Request:
    def __init__(self, rid, arrival, prompt_len, output_len):
        self.rid = rid
        self.arrival = arrival
        self.prompt_len = prompt_len
        self.output_len = output_len
        self.cached = 0
        self.generated = 0
        self.token_times = []

    def done(self):
        return self.generated >= self.output_len


def make_requests(seed, n, rate, prompt=(64, 512), output=(16, 256)):
    rng = random.Random(seed)
    t = 0.0
    out = []
    for rid in range(n):
        t += rng.expovariate(rate)
        out.append(Request(rid, round(t, 6), rng.randint(*prompt), rng.randint(*output)))
    return out


def run_server(model, hw, requests, schedule):
    pending = sorted(requests, key=lambda r: (r.arrival, r.rid))
    queue, running, finished, log = [], [], [], []
    now, i = 0.0, 0
    while i < len(pending) or queue or running:
        while i < len(pending) and pending[i].arrival <= now:
            queue.append(pending[i])
            i += 1
        plan = schedule(queue, running, now) if queue or running else []
        if not plan:
            if i < len(pending):
                now = max(now, pending[i].arrival)
                continue
            if queue or running:
                raise RuntimeError("requests are waiting but the scheduler planned nothing")
            break
        for req, n in plan:
            if req in queue:
                queue.remove(req)
                running.append(req)
        now += step_cost(model, hw, [(n, req.cached) for req, n in plan])["time"]
        for req, n in plan:
            req.cached += n
            if req.cached >= req.prompt_len:
                req.generated += 1
                req.token_times.append(now)
        log.append((now, len(plan), sum(n for _, n in plan), sum(r.cached for r in running)))
        for req in [r for r in running if r.done()]:
            running.remove(req)
            finished.append(req)
    return finished, log


def one_at_a_time(queue, running, now):
    if running:
        return [(running[0], 1)]
    if queue:
        return [(queue[0], queue[0].prompt_len)]
    return []


def continuous(max_batch, max_tokens):
    def schedule(queue, running, now):
        plan = [(req, 1) for req in running]
        tokens = len(plan)
        for req in queue:
            if len(plan) >= max_batch or tokens + req.prompt_len > max_tokens:
                break
            plan.append((req, req.prompt_len))
            tokens += req.prompt_len
        return plan

    return schedule


def percentile(values, p):
    ordered = sorted(values)
    return ordered[max(0, math.ceil(p / 100 * len(ordered)) - 1)]


def serving_metrics(finished, ttft_slo, tpot_slo):
    ttft = [r.token_times[0] - r.arrival for r in finished]
    tpot = {r.rid: (r.token_times[-1] - r.token_times[0]) / (r.output_len - 1)
            for r in finished if r.output_len > 1}
    start = min(r.arrival for r in finished)
    end = max(r.token_times[-1] for r in finished)
    duration = end - start
    tokens = sum(r.output_len for r in finished)
    good = sum(1 for r, t in zip(finished, ttft) if t <= ttft_slo and tpot.get(r.rid, 0.0) <= tpot_slo)
    tpots = list(tpot.values())
    return {
        "ttft_p50": round(percentile(ttft, 50), 6),
        "ttft_p99": round(percentile(ttft, 99), 6),
        "tpot_p50": round(percentile(tpots, 50), 6) if tpots else None,
        "tpot_p99": round(percentile(tpots, 99), 6) if tpots else None,
        "throughput": round(tokens / duration, 6),
        "goodput": round(good / duration, 6),
    }


def meets(req, ttft_slo, tpot_slo):
    if req.token_times[0] - req.arrival > ttft_slo:
        return False
    if req.output_len > 1:
        tpot = (req.token_times[-1] - req.token_times[0]) / (req.output_len - 1)
        return tpot <= tpot_slo
    return True


def capacity(model, hw, rates, n, seed, ttft_slo, tpot_slo, target):
    best, fractions = None, []
    for rate in rates:
        finished, _ = run_server(model, hw, make_requests(seed, n, rate), continuous(32, 2048))
        frac = sum(meets(r, ttft_slo, tpot_slo) for r in finished) / len(finished)
        fractions.append(round(frac, 3))
        if frac >= target and (best is None or rate > best):
            best = rate
    return (best, fractions)
--- hint
Write a helper `meets(req, ttft_slo, tpot_slo)` that returns whether one finished request met both objectives, treating one-token answers as meeting the TPOT one.
--- hint
For each rate, run the server on a new `make_requests(seed, n, rate)`, count the requests that meet the SLO, and divide by n. Keep the largest rate whose fraction reaches `target`; the rates may come in any order.
--- check case | The knee lies between 10 and 20 requests a second
capacity(Model(7_000_000_000, 32, 4096, 8, 128), Accel(3e14, 8.64e11, 48e9), [5.0, 10.0, 15.0, 20.0], 200, 3, 0.5, 0.03, 0.9)
=> (10.0, [1.0, 1.0, 0.45, 0.25])
--- check case | Rates in any order
capacity(Model(7_000_000_000, 32, 4096, 8, 128), Accel(3e14, 8.64e11, 48e9), [20.0, 5.0, 10.0], 200, 3, 0.5, 0.03, 0.9)[0]
=> 10.0
--- check case | An objective nobody can meet
capacity(Model(7_000_000_000, 32, 4096, 8, 128), Accel(3e14, 8.64e11, 48e9), [1.0, 2.0], 50, 3, 0.5, 0.01, 0.5)
=> (None, [0.0, 0.0])
--- check case | A bigger accelerator moves the knee
capacity(Model(7_000_000_000, 32, 4096, 8, 128), Accel(1e15, 3.35e12, 80e9), [10.0, 20.0, 40.0], 200, 3, 0.5, 0.03, 0.9)[0]
=> 40.0

+++ practice | Admission under a memory budget
--- task
A real scheduler must also respect KV memory. Write `continuous_kv(max_batch, kv_tokens)`, which returns a scheduler like the lesson's `continuous`, but with a memory limit instead of a token limit, and a list of rejected requests:

- It reserves memory for each request's whole life when admitting it: `prompt_len + output_len` tokens. The reservations of the running requests plus the new one must be at most `kv_tokens`. (Reserving the worst case means nobody is ever preempted.)
- Each step: one decode for every running request; then admit from the front of the queue while the plan has fewer than `max_batch` entries and the reservation fits, each with its whole prompt. Stop at the first request that does not fit.
- A request whose own reservation is bigger than `kv_tokens` can **never** fit, and waiting for it would block the queue forever. When it reaches the front of the queue, remove it from the queue (`queue.remove(req)`), append it to the list, and carry on with the next request.

`continuous_kv` returns a pair `(schedule, rejected)`, where `rejected` is the list the scheduler appends to.
--- starter


import math
import random


class Model:
    """The shapes of a decoder-only transformer that set what serving it costs."""

    def __init__(self, n_params, n_layers, d_model, n_kv_heads, head_dim, bytes_per_param=2, kv_bytes=2):
        self.n_params = n_params
        self.n_layers = n_layers
        self.d_model = d_model
        self.n_kv_heads = n_kv_heads
        self.head_dim = head_dim
        self.bytes_per_param = bytes_per_param
        self.kv_bytes = kv_bytes


class Accel:
    """One accelerator: peak FLOP/s, memory bandwidth in bytes/s, memory size in bytes."""

    def __init__(self, peak_flops, mem_bw, mem_bytes):
        self.peak_flops = peak_flops
        self.mem_bw = mem_bw
        self.mem_bytes = mem_bytes


def kv_bytes_per_token(model):
    return 2 * model.n_layers * model.n_kv_heads * model.head_dim * model.kv_bytes


def step_cost(model, hw, work):
    if not work:
        return {"flops": 0, "bytes": 0, "time": 0.0, "bound": "idle"}
    tokens = sum(n for n, _ in work)
    keys = sum(n * c + n * (n + 1) // 2 for n, c in work)
    flops = 2 * model.n_params * tokens + 4 * model.n_layers * model.d_model * keys
    kv = kv_bytes_per_token(model)
    moved = model.n_params * model.bytes_per_param + kv * sum(c + n for n, c in work)
    t_compute = flops / hw.peak_flops
    t_memory = moved / hw.mem_bw
    bound = "compute" if t_compute > t_memory else "memory"
    return {"flops": flops, "bytes": moved, "time": max(t_compute, t_memory), "bound": bound}


class Request:
    def __init__(self, rid, arrival, prompt_len, output_len):
        self.rid = rid
        self.arrival = arrival
        self.prompt_len = prompt_len
        self.output_len = output_len
        self.cached = 0
        self.generated = 0
        self.token_times = []

    def done(self):
        return self.generated >= self.output_len


def make_requests(seed, n, rate, prompt=(64, 512), output=(16, 256)):
    rng = random.Random(seed)
    t = 0.0
    out = []
    for rid in range(n):
        t += rng.expovariate(rate)
        out.append(Request(rid, round(t, 6), rng.randint(*prompt), rng.randint(*output)))
    return out


def run_server(model, hw, requests, schedule):
    pending = sorted(requests, key=lambda r: (r.arrival, r.rid))
    queue, running, finished, log = [], [], [], []
    now, i = 0.0, 0
    while i < len(pending) or queue or running:
        while i < len(pending) and pending[i].arrival <= now:
            queue.append(pending[i])
            i += 1
        plan = schedule(queue, running, now) if queue or running else []
        if not plan:
            if i < len(pending):
                now = max(now, pending[i].arrival)
                continue
            if queue or running:
                raise RuntimeError("requests are waiting but the scheduler planned nothing")
            break
        for req, n in plan:
            if req in queue:
                queue.remove(req)
                running.append(req)
        now += step_cost(model, hw, [(n, req.cached) for req, n in plan])["time"]
        for req, n in plan:
            req.cached += n
            if req.cached >= req.prompt_len:
                req.generated += 1
                req.token_times.append(now)
        log.append((now, len(plan), sum(n for _, n in plan), sum(r.cached for r in running)))
        for req in [r for r in running if r.done()]:
            running.remove(req)
            finished.append(req)
    return finished, log


def one_at_a_time(queue, running, now):
    if running:
        return [(running[0], 1)]
    if queue:
        return [(queue[0], queue[0].prompt_len)]
    return []


def continuous_kv(max_batch, kv_tokens):
    rejected = []

    def schedule(queue, running, now):
        plan = [(req, 1) for req in running]
        for req in queue:
            if len(plan) >= max_batch:
                break
            plan.append((req, req.prompt_len))
        return plan

    return schedule, rejected
--- solution


import math
import random


class Model:
    """The shapes of a decoder-only transformer that set what serving it costs."""

    def __init__(self, n_params, n_layers, d_model, n_kv_heads, head_dim, bytes_per_param=2, kv_bytes=2):
        self.n_params = n_params
        self.n_layers = n_layers
        self.d_model = d_model
        self.n_kv_heads = n_kv_heads
        self.head_dim = head_dim
        self.bytes_per_param = bytes_per_param
        self.kv_bytes = kv_bytes


class Accel:
    """One accelerator: peak FLOP/s, memory bandwidth in bytes/s, memory size in bytes."""

    def __init__(self, peak_flops, mem_bw, mem_bytes):
        self.peak_flops = peak_flops
        self.mem_bw = mem_bw
        self.mem_bytes = mem_bytes


def kv_bytes_per_token(model):
    return 2 * model.n_layers * model.n_kv_heads * model.head_dim * model.kv_bytes


def step_cost(model, hw, work):
    if not work:
        return {"flops": 0, "bytes": 0, "time": 0.0, "bound": "idle"}
    tokens = sum(n for n, _ in work)
    keys = sum(n * c + n * (n + 1) // 2 for n, c in work)
    flops = 2 * model.n_params * tokens + 4 * model.n_layers * model.d_model * keys
    kv = kv_bytes_per_token(model)
    moved = model.n_params * model.bytes_per_param + kv * sum(c + n for n, c in work)
    t_compute = flops / hw.peak_flops
    t_memory = moved / hw.mem_bw
    bound = "compute" if t_compute > t_memory else "memory"
    return {"flops": flops, "bytes": moved, "time": max(t_compute, t_memory), "bound": bound}


class Request:
    def __init__(self, rid, arrival, prompt_len, output_len):
        self.rid = rid
        self.arrival = arrival
        self.prompt_len = prompt_len
        self.output_len = output_len
        self.cached = 0
        self.generated = 0
        self.token_times = []

    def done(self):
        return self.generated >= self.output_len


def make_requests(seed, n, rate, prompt=(64, 512), output=(16, 256)):
    rng = random.Random(seed)
    t = 0.0
    out = []
    for rid in range(n):
        t += rng.expovariate(rate)
        out.append(Request(rid, round(t, 6), rng.randint(*prompt), rng.randint(*output)))
    return out


def run_server(model, hw, requests, schedule):
    pending = sorted(requests, key=lambda r: (r.arrival, r.rid))
    queue, running, finished, log = [], [], [], []
    now, i = 0.0, 0
    while i < len(pending) or queue or running:
        while i < len(pending) and pending[i].arrival <= now:
            queue.append(pending[i])
            i += 1
        plan = schedule(queue, running, now) if queue or running else []
        if not plan:
            if i < len(pending):
                now = max(now, pending[i].arrival)
                continue
            if queue or running:
                raise RuntimeError("requests are waiting but the scheduler planned nothing")
            break
        for req, n in plan:
            if req in queue:
                queue.remove(req)
                running.append(req)
        now += step_cost(model, hw, [(n, req.cached) for req, n in plan])["time"]
        for req, n in plan:
            req.cached += n
            if req.cached >= req.prompt_len:
                req.generated += 1
                req.token_times.append(now)
        log.append((now, len(plan), sum(n for _, n in plan), sum(r.cached for r in running)))
        for req in [r for r in running if r.done()]:
            running.remove(req)
            finished.append(req)
    return finished, log


def one_at_a_time(queue, running, now):
    if running:
        return [(running[0], 1)]
    if queue:
        return [(queue[0], queue[0].prompt_len)]
    return []


def continuous_kv(max_batch, kv_tokens):
    rejected = []

    def schedule(queue, running, now):
        plan = [(req, 1) for req in running]
        reserved = sum(r.prompt_len + r.output_len for r in running)
        i = 0
        while i < len(queue) and len(plan) < max_batch:
            req = queue[i]
            need = req.prompt_len + req.output_len
            if need > kv_tokens:
                queue.remove(req)
                rejected.append(req)
                continue
            if reserved + need > kv_tokens:
                break
            plan.append((req, req.prompt_len))
            reserved += need
            i += 1
        return plan

    return schedule, rejected
--- hint
Start the reservation total from the running requests: the sum of `prompt_len + output_len`. Then walk the queue with an index `i` in a `while` loop rather than a `for` loop, because you may remove requests from the queue as you go.
--- hint
A request that is bigger than `kv_tokens` on its own is removed and recorded, and the loop goes on. One that only does not fit **now** stops the loop. A planned request stays in the queue (the server moves it to `running` itself), so step past it with `i += 1`; after removing a rejected one, do not move `i`.
--- check case | Memory, not batch size, limits the batch
(lambda sr: (lambda log: [n for _, n, _, _ in log])(run_server(Model(7_000_000_000, 32, 4096, 8, 128), Accel(3e14, 8.64e11, 48e9), [Request(i, 0.0, 100, 2) for i in range(5)], sr[0])[1]))(continuous_kv(8, 250))
=> [2, 2, 2, 2, 1, 1]
--- check case | A request too big for memory is rejected instead of blocking the queue
(lambda sr: (lambda f: ([r.rid for r in sorted(f, key=lambda r: r.rid)], [r.rid for r in sr[1]]))(run_server(Model(7_000_000_000, 32, 4096, 8, 128), Accel(3e14, 8.64e11, 48e9), [Request(0, 0.0, 100, 2), Request(1, 0.0, 900, 200), Request(2, 0.0, 100, 2)], sr[0])[0]))(continuous_kv(8, 1000))
=> ([0, 2], [1])
--- check case | A request that only does not fit yet waits its turn
(lambda sr: (lambda log: [t for _, _, t, _ in log])(run_server(Model(7_000_000_000, 32, 4096, 8, 128), Accel(3e14, 8.64e11, 48e9), [Request(0, 0.0, 300, 3), Request(1, 0.0, 600, 2), Request(2, 0.0, 10, 2)], sr[0])[1]))(continuous_kv(8, 900))
=> [300, 1, 1, 610, 2]
?? Request 1 needs 602 tokens on top of request 0's 303, which is over 900, so it waits, and request 2 waits behind it.
--- check test | On 150 requests, the reservations never exceed memory and none is rejected
(lambda sr: (lambda f: len(f) == 150 and not sr[1])(run_server(Model(7_000_000_000, 32, 4096, 8, 128), Accel(3e14, 8.64e11, 48e9), make_requests(4, 150, 12.0), (lambda inner: lambda q, run, now: (lambda plan: plan if sum(r.prompt_len + r.output_len for r, _ in plan) <= 20000 else 1 / 0)(inner(q, run, now)))(sr[0]))[0]))(continuous_kv(64, 20000))
--- check case | When nothing fits at all, everything is rejected and the run ends
(lambda sr: ([r.rid for r in run_server(Model(7_000_000_000, 32, 4096, 8, 128), Accel(3e14, 8.64e11, 48e9), [Request(0, 0.0, 100, 2), Request(1, 0.0, 200, 2)], sr[0])[0]], [r.rid for r in sr[1]]))(continuous_kv(8, 50))
=> ([], [0, 1])

+++ practice | Debug: the batch that grows past its limit
--- task
This continuous batching scheduler should never put more than `max_batch` requests in one step. Under a burst of arrivals it does: with `max_batch` 4, two requests running and five queued, it plans 7 requests in one step instead of 4. Find the bug and fix it. The rest of its behaviour (decode every running request, then admit from the front of the queue in order while there is room, stopping at the first that does not fit the token budget) is right.
--- starter

--- solution


import math
import random


class Model:
    """The shapes of a decoder-only transformer that set what serving it costs."""

    def __init__(self, n_params, n_layers, d_model, n_kv_heads, head_dim, bytes_per_param=2, kv_bytes=2):
        self.n_params = n_params
        self.n_layers = n_layers
        self.d_model = d_model
        self.n_kv_heads = n_kv_heads
        self.head_dim = head_dim
        self.bytes_per_param = bytes_per_param
        self.kv_bytes = kv_bytes


class Accel:
    """One accelerator: peak FLOP/s, memory bandwidth in bytes/s, memory size in bytes."""

    def __init__(self, peak_flops, mem_bw, mem_bytes):
        self.peak_flops = peak_flops
        self.mem_bw = mem_bw
        self.mem_bytes = mem_bytes


def kv_bytes_per_token(model):
    return 2 * model.n_layers * model.n_kv_heads * model.head_dim * model.kv_bytes


def step_cost(model, hw, work):
    if not work:
        return {"flops": 0, "bytes": 0, "time": 0.0, "bound": "idle"}
    tokens = sum(n for n, _ in work)
    keys = sum(n * c + n * (n + 1) // 2 for n, c in work)
    flops = 2 * model.n_params * tokens + 4 * model.n_layers * model.d_model * keys

    moved = model.n_params * model.bytes_per_param + kv * sum(c + n for n, c in work)
    t_compute = flops / hw.peak_flops
    t_memory = moved / hw.mem_bw
    bound = "compute" if t_compute > t_memory else "memory"
    return {"flops": flops, "bytes": moved, "time": max(t_compute, t_memory), "bound": bound}

    def __init__(self, rid, arrival, prompt_len, output_len):
        self.rid = rid
        self.arrival = arrival
        self.prompt_len = prompt_len
        self.output_len = output_len

        self.generated = 0

        out.append(Request(rid, round(t, 6), rng.randint(*prompt), rng.randint(*output)))
    return out

            queue.append(pending[i])
            i += 1
        plan = schedule(queue, running, now) if queue or running else []
        if not plan:

                now = max(now, pending[i].arrival)
                continue

                raise RuntimeError("requests are waiting but the scheduler planned nothing")
            break
        for req, n in plan:
            if req in queue:
                queue.remove(req)
                running.append(req)
        now += step_cost(model, hw, [(n, req.cached) for req, n in plan])["time"]
        for req, n in plan:
            req.cached += n
            if req.cached >= req.prompt_len:
                req.generated += 1
                req.token_times.append(now)
        log.append((now, len(plan), sum(n for _, n in plan), sum(r.cached for r in running)))
        for req in [r for r in running if r.done()]:
            running.remove(req)
            finished.append(req)
    return finished, log


def one_at_a_time(queue, running, now):
    if running:
        return [(running[0], 1)]
    if queue:
        return [(queue[0], queue[0].prompt_len)]
    return []


def continuous(max_batch, max_tokens):

        plan = [(req, 1) for req in running]
        tokens = len(plan)
        for req in queue:
            if len(plan) >= max_batch or tokens + req.prompt_len > max_tokens:

            plan.append((req, req.prompt_len))
            tokens += req.prompt_len
        return plan

    return schedule


--- hint
The limit is checked against `running`. Does `running` change while the loop adds requests to the plan?
--- hint
The server only moves planned requests into `running` after the scheduler returns. Count the plan, `len(plan)`, which holds both the running requests and the ones admitted so far.
--- check case | The burst from the task
(lambda run, q: len(continuous(4, 4096)(q, run, 0.0)))([Request(i, 0.0, 10, 5) for i in range(2)], [Request(i, 0.0, 10, 5) for i in range(2, 7)])
=> 4
--- check case | An empty server admits exactly max_batch
(lambda q: [r.rid for r, _ in continuous(3, 4096)(q, [], 0.0)])([Request(i, 0.0, 10, 5) for i in range(6)])
=> [0, 1, 2]
--- check case | The token budget still stops admission
(lambda q: [n for _, n in continuous(8, 100)(q, [], 0.0)])([Request(i, 0.0, 40, 5) for i in range(4)])
=> [40, 40]
--- check test | A whole run never exceeds max_batch
max(n for _, n, _, _ in run_server(Model(7_000_000_000, 32, 4096, 8, 128), Accel(3e14, 8.64e11, 48e9), make_requests(2, 120, 30.0), continuous(6, 4096))[1]) == 6

+++ practice | Chunked prefill: no more stutter
--- task
When a long prompt joins a step, every running request waits for that whole prefill before its next token. **Chunked prefill** splits prompts across steps. Write `chunked(max_batch, budget)`, which returns a scheduler that plans each step like this, with at most `budget` tokens in the step:

1. every running request whose prompt is done (`cached >= prompt_len`) decodes one token, `(req, 1)`;
2. every running request still in prefill, oldest first (the order of `running`), gets the next chunk of its prompt: `min(tokens left in the budget, prompt_len − cached)` tokens, skipped if that is 0;
3. requests from the front of the queue join while the plan has fewer than `max_batch` entries and some budget is left, each with `min(budget left, prompt_len)` tokens.

`run_server` handles the rest: a request emits its first token only in the step where its `cached` reaches `prompt_len`. `budget` is larger than `max_batch`, so decodes always fit.

The checks run a workload with long prompts both ways and compare the p99 TPOT, using the lesson's `continuous` and `serving_metrics` from the starter.
--- starter


import math
import random


class Model:
    """The shapes of a decoder-only transformer that set what serving it costs."""

    def __init__(self, n_params, n_layers, d_model, n_kv_heads, head_dim, bytes_per_param=2, kv_bytes=2):
        self.n_params = n_params
        self.n_layers = n_layers
        self.d_model = d_model
        self.n_kv_heads = n_kv_heads
        self.head_dim = head_dim
        self.bytes_per_param = bytes_per_param
        self.kv_bytes = kv_bytes


class Accel:
    """One accelerator: peak FLOP/s, memory bandwidth in bytes/s, memory size in bytes."""

    def __init__(self, peak_flops, mem_bw, mem_bytes):
        self.peak_flops = peak_flops
        self.mem_bw = mem_bw
        self.mem_bytes = mem_bytes


def kv_bytes_per_token(model):
    return 2 * model.n_layers * model.n_kv_heads * model.head_dim * model.kv_bytes


def step_cost(model, hw, work):
    if not work:
        return {"flops": 0, "bytes": 0, "time": 0.0, "bound": "idle"}
    tokens = sum(n for n, _ in work)
    keys = sum(n * c + n * (n + 1) // 2 for n, c in work)
    flops = 2 * model.n_params * tokens + 4 * model.n_layers * model.d_model * keys
    kv = kv_bytes_per_token(model)
    moved = model.n_params * model.bytes_per_param + kv * sum(c + n for n, c in work)
    t_compute = flops / hw.peak_flops
    t_memory = moved / hw.mem_bw
    bound = "compute" if t_compute > t_memory else "memory"
    return {"flops": flops, "bytes": moved, "time": max(t_compute, t_memory), "bound": bound}


class Request:
    def __init__(self, rid, arrival, prompt_len, output_len):
        self.rid = rid
        self.arrival = arrival
        self.prompt_len = prompt_len
        self.output_len = output_len
        self.cached = 0
        self.generated = 0
        self.token_times = []

    def done(self):
        return self.generated >= self.output_len


def make_requests(seed, n, rate, prompt=(64, 512), output=(16, 256)):
    rng = random.Random(seed)
    t = 0.0
    out = []
    for rid in range(n):
        t += rng.expovariate(rate)
        out.append(Request(rid, round(t, 6), rng.randint(*prompt), rng.randint(*output)))
    return out


def run_server(model, hw, requests, schedule):
    pending = sorted(requests, key=lambda r: (r.arrival, r.rid))
    queue, running, finished, log = [], [], [], []
    now, i = 0.0, 0
    while i < len(pending) or queue or running:
        while i < len(pending) and pending[i].arrival <= now:
            queue.append(pending[i])
            i += 1
        plan = schedule(queue, running, now) if queue or running else []
        if not plan:
            if i < len(pending):
                now = max(now, pending[i].arrival)
                continue
            if queue or running:
                raise RuntimeError("requests are waiting but the scheduler planned nothing")
            break
        for req, n in plan:
            if req in queue:
                queue.remove(req)
                running.append(req)
        now += step_cost(model, hw, [(n, req.cached) for req, n in plan])["time"]
        for req, n in plan:
            req.cached += n
            if req.cached >= req.prompt_len:
                req.generated += 1
                req.token_times.append(now)
        log.append((now, len(plan), sum(n for _, n in plan), sum(r.cached for r in running)))
        for req in [r for r in running if r.done()]:
            running.remove(req)
            finished.append(req)
    return finished, log


def one_at_a_time(queue, running, now):
    if running:
        return [(running[0], 1)]
    if queue:
        return [(queue[0], queue[0].prompt_len)]
    return []


def continuous(max_batch, max_tokens):
    def schedule(queue, running, now):
        plan = [(req, 1) for req in running]
        tokens = len(plan)
        for req in queue:
            if len(plan) >= max_batch or tokens + req.prompt_len > max_tokens:
                break
            plan.append((req, req.prompt_len))
            tokens += req.prompt_len
        return plan

    return schedule


def percentile(values, p):
    ordered = sorted(values)
    return ordered[max(0, math.ceil(p / 100 * len(ordered)) - 1)]


def serving_metrics(finished, ttft_slo, tpot_slo):
    ttft = [r.token_times[0] - r.arrival for r in finished]
    tpot = {r.rid: (r.token_times[-1] - r.token_times[0]) / (r.output_len - 1)
            for r in finished if r.output_len > 1}
    start = min(r.arrival for r in finished)
    end = max(r.token_times[-1] for r in finished)
    duration = end - start
    tokens = sum(r.output_len for r in finished)
    good = sum(1 for r, t in zip(finished, ttft) if t <= ttft_slo and tpot.get(r.rid, 0.0) <= tpot_slo)
    tpots = list(tpot.values())
    return {
        "ttft_p50": round(percentile(ttft, 50), 6),
        "ttft_p99": round(percentile(ttft, 99), 6),
        "tpot_p50": round(percentile(tpots, 50), 6) if tpots else None,
        "tpot_p99": round(percentile(tpots, 99), 6) if tpots else None,
        "throughput": round(tokens / duration, 6),
        "goodput": round(good / duration, 6),
    }


def chunked(max_batch, budget):
    def schedule(queue, running, now):
        return continuous(max_batch, budget)(queue, running, now)

    return schedule
--- solution


import math
import random


class Model:
    """The shapes of a decoder-only transformer that set what serving it costs."""

    def __init__(self, n_params, n_layers, d_model, n_kv_heads, head_dim, bytes_per_param=2, kv_bytes=2):
        self.n_params = n_params
        self.n_layers = n_layers
        self.d_model = d_model
        self.n_kv_heads = n_kv_heads
        self.head_dim = head_dim
        self.bytes_per_param = bytes_per_param
        self.kv_bytes = kv_bytes


class Accel:
    """One accelerator: peak FLOP/s, memory bandwidth in bytes/s, memory size in bytes."""

    def __init__(self, peak_flops, mem_bw, mem_bytes):
        self.peak_flops = peak_flops
        self.mem_bw = mem_bw
        self.mem_bytes = mem_bytes


def kv_bytes_per_token(model):
    return 2 * model.n_layers * model.n_kv_heads * model.head_dim * model.kv_bytes


def step_cost(model, hw, work):
    if not work:
        return {"flops": 0, "bytes": 0, "time": 0.0, "bound": "idle"}
    tokens = sum(n for n, _ in work)
    keys = sum(n * c + n * (n + 1) // 2 for n, c in work)
    flops = 2 * model.n_params * tokens + 4 * model.n_layers * model.d_model * keys
    kv = kv_bytes_per_token(model)
    moved = model.n_params * model.bytes_per_param + kv * sum(c + n for n, c in work)
    t_compute = flops / hw.peak_flops
    t_memory = moved / hw.mem_bw
    bound = "compute" if t_compute > t_memory else "memory"
    return {"flops": flops, "bytes": moved, "time": max(t_compute, t_memory), "bound": bound}


class Request:
    def __init__(self, rid, arrival, prompt_len, output_len):
        self.rid = rid
        self.arrival = arrival
        self.prompt_len = prompt_len
        self.output_len = output_len
        self.cached = 0
        self.generated = 0
        self.token_times = []

    def done(self):
        return self.generated >= self.output_len


def make_requests(seed, n, rate, prompt=(64, 512), output=(16, 256)):
    rng = random.Random(seed)
    t = 0.0
    out = []
    for rid in range(n):
        t += rng.expovariate(rate)
        out.append(Request(rid, round(t, 6), rng.randint(*prompt), rng.randint(*output)))
    return out


def run_server(model, hw, requests, schedule):
    pending = sorted(requests, key=lambda r: (r.arrival, r.rid))
    queue, running, finished, log = [], [], [], []
    now, i = 0.0, 0
    while i < len(pending) or queue or running:
        while i < len(pending) and pending[i].arrival <= now:
            queue.append(pending[i])
            i += 1
        plan = schedule(queue, running, now) if queue or running else []
        if not plan:
            if i < len(pending):
                now = max(now, pending[i].arrival)
                continue
            if queue or running:
                raise RuntimeError("requests are waiting but the scheduler planned nothing")
            break
        for req, n in plan:
            if req in queue:
                queue.remove(req)
                running.append(req)
        now += step_cost(model, hw, [(n, req.cached) for req, n in plan])["time"]
        for req, n in plan:
            req.cached += n
            if req.cached >= req.prompt_len:
                req.generated += 1
                req.token_times.append(now)
        log.append((now, len(plan), sum(n for _, n in plan), sum(r.cached for r in running)))
        for req in [r for r in running if r.done()]:
            running.remove(req)
            finished.append(req)
    return finished, log


def one_at_a_time(queue, running, now):
    if running:
        return [(running[0], 1)]
    if queue:
        return [(queue[0], queue[0].prompt_len)]
    return []


def continuous(max_batch, max_tokens):
    def schedule(queue, running, now):
        plan = [(req, 1) for req in running]
        tokens = len(plan)
        for req in queue:
            if len(plan) >= max_batch or tokens + req.prompt_len > max_tokens:
                break
            plan.append((req, req.prompt_len))
            tokens += req.prompt_len
        return plan

    return schedule


def percentile(values, p):
    ordered = sorted(values)
    return ordered[max(0, math.ceil(p / 100 * len(ordered)) - 1)]


def serving_metrics(finished, ttft_slo, tpot_slo):
    ttft = [r.token_times[0] - r.arrival for r in finished]
    tpot = {r.rid: (r.token_times[-1] - r.token_times[0]) / (r.output_len - 1)
            for r in finished if r.output_len > 1}
    start = min(r.arrival for r in finished)
    end = max(r.token_times[-1] for r in finished)
    duration = end - start
    tokens = sum(r.output_len for r in finished)
    good = sum(1 for r, t in zip(finished, ttft) if t <= ttft_slo and tpot.get(r.rid, 0.0) <= tpot_slo)
    tpots = list(tpot.values())
    return {
        "ttft_p50": round(percentile(ttft, 50), 6),
        "ttft_p99": round(percentile(ttft, 99), 6),
        "tpot_p50": round(percentile(tpots, 50), 6) if tpots else None,
        "tpot_p99": round(percentile(tpots, 99), 6) if tpots else None,
        "throughput": round(tokens / duration, 6),
        "goodput": round(good / duration, 6),
    }


def chunked(max_batch, budget):
    def schedule(queue, running, now):
        plan = [(req, 1) for req in running if req.cached >= req.prompt_len]
        left = budget - len(plan)
        for req in running:
            if req.cached < req.prompt_len and left > 0:
                n = min(left, req.prompt_len - req.cached)
                plan.append((req, n))
                left -= n
        for req in queue:
            if len(plan) >= max_batch or left <= 0:
                break
            n = min(left, req.prompt_len)
            plan.append((req, n))
            left -= n
        return plan

    return schedule
--- hint
Keep a count `left` of the budget still free. Decodes go first, one token each. Then partly prefilled running requests take what they can, then new requests.
--- hint
A request mid-prefill has `req.cached < req.prompt_len`; its next chunk is `min(left, req.prompt_len - req.cached)`. A new request's first chunk is `min(left, req.prompt_len)`. Stop admitting when the plan is full or `left` reaches 0.
--- check case | A 1,000-token prompt with a budget of 400 is prefilled in three steps, next to a running decode
(lambda log: [t for _, _, t, _ in log])(run_server(Model(7_000_000_000, 32, 4096, 8, 128), Accel(3e14, 8.64e11, 48e9), [Request(0, 0.0, 10, 5), Request(1, 0.001, 1000, 2)], chunked(8, 400))[1])
=> [10, 400, 400, 203, 2]
--- check case | Its first token comes only when its whole prompt is in
(lambda f: [(r.rid, len(r.token_times)) for r in sorted(f, key=lambda r: r.rid)])(run_server(Model(7_000_000_000, 32, 4096, 8, 128), Accel(3e14, 8.64e11, 48e9), [Request(0, 0.0, 10, 5), Request(1, 0.001, 1000, 2)], chunked(8, 400))[0])
=> [(0, 5), (1, 2)]
--- check test | No step goes over the budget on a long-prompt workload
max(t for _, _, t, _ in run_server(Model(7_000_000_000, 32, 4096, 8, 128), Accel(3e14, 8.64e11, 48e9), make_requests(6, 150, 6.0, prompt=(500, 4000)), chunked(32, 512))[1]) <= 512
--- check test | Chunking cuts the p99 TPOT on long prompts
(lambda c, k: k["tpot_p99"] < 0.8 * c["tpot_p99"])(*[serving_metrics(run_server(Model(7_000_000_000, 32, 4096, 8, 128), Accel(3e14, 8.64e11, 48e9), make_requests(6, 150, 6.0, prompt=(500, 4000)), sch)[0], 2.0, 0.05) for sch in (continuous(32, 4096), chunked(32, 512))])
--- check case | The p99 TPOT both ways, in milliseconds
tuple(round(serving_metrics(run_server(Model(7_000_000_000, 32, 4096, 8, 128), Accel(3e14, 8.64e11, 48e9), make_requests(6, 150, 6.0, prompt=(500, 4000)), sch)[0], 2.0, 0.05)["tpot_p99"] * 1000, 1) for sch in (continuous(32, 4096), chunked(32, 512)))
=> (83.1, 25.4)

=== mlsys2-04 | Sampling and speculative decoding
--- teach
Lesson 3's scheduler keeps the batch full, but each request still gets one token per step, and lesson 1 showed why a step is slow: it waits on memory, with most of the compute idle. This lesson first pins down how a token is chosen from the model's output, and then uses that idle compute to produce **several** tokens per step without changing what the model would have written.

### From logits to a token

A forward pass ends with **logits**: one score per token of the vocabulary. Your bigram model turned counts into probabilities and drew with `rng.choices`; a transformer turns logits into probabilities with softmax, as in the Python AI course. How the next token is picked from them is the **sampling** policy, and servers let each request choose it.

- **Greedy**: take the token with the highest probability. Repeatable, but often dull and repetitive.
- **Temperature** T: divide every logit by T before the softmax. T below 1 sharpens the distribution towards the favourite; T above 1 flattens it. As T approaches 0 it becomes greedy, so servers treat T = 0 as greedy rather than dividing by zero.
- **Top-k**: keep only the k most likely tokens, set the rest to probability 0, and rescale the survivors to add up to 1.
- **Top-p** (nucleus sampling): keep the smallest set of most likely tokens whose probabilities add up to at least p, then rescale. When the model is sure, that set is one or two tokens; when it is unsure, it is many.

Here is temperature on three logits:

```python
import math

logits = [2.0, 1.0, 0.0]
for t in (1.0, 0.5):
    exps = [math.exp(x / t) for x in logits]
    print(t, [round(e / sum(exps), 3) for e in exps])
# 1.0 [0.665, 0.245, 0.09]
# 0.5 [0.867, 0.117, 0.016]
```

**Determinism.** A sampled answer is repeatable only if the random draws are: a server that accepts a seed per request seeds a generator for that request alone, as your bigram sampler did with `random.Random(0)`. Even then, the logits themselves can differ in the last bits between runs, because the batch a request shares changes how the arithmetic is grouped, a problem the note on [[batch invariance|batch-invariance]] explains.

### Speculative decoding: guess, then check

Lesson 1's numbers: a decode step for one token reads 14 GB of weights to do 14 GFLOP. A step that processes 5 tokens of the same request reads the same 14 GB and does 70 GFLOP, still far below the ridge, so it takes about the same time. Running the big model on 5 positions costs about as much as running it on 1.

**Speculative decoding** turns that into speed:

1. A small, fast **[[draft model|draft-models]]** proposes the next k tokens, one at a time (cheap: it might be 50 times smaller).
2. The big **target model** runs **once** over those k positions. In that single pass it computes its own distribution p at every position: what it would have predicted after the prefix, after the prefix plus the first guess, and so on.
3. A rule decides how many guesses to keep. Each step yields between 1 and k + 1 tokens for the price of one target pass plus k draft passes.

The whole trick is in step 3. A naive rule, such as "keep a guess if the target also likes it", would change the output: the text would drift towards what the draft model likes. The [[rule below|rejection-sampling]] keeps the output distribution **exactly** the target's.

### The acceptance rule

Walk the guesses in order. For guess x, the draft gave it probability q(x) and the target p(x).

- **Accept** x with probability min(1, p(x) / q(x)). If the target likes x at least as much as the draft, it is always kept; if the target likes it half as much, it is kept half the time.
- On the first **rejection**, throw away x and every later guess. In its place draw one token from the **residual distribution**: r(y) = max(0, p(y) − q(y)) / Z, where Z is the sum of max(0, p(y) − q(y)) over all y. The step ends there.
- If all k guesses are accepted, draw one **bonus** token from the target's distribution after the last guess: the target pass computed it anyway.

### Why the output is exactly the target's

Look at one position. Write m(y) = min(p(y), q(y)), and β = the sum of m(y) over all y: the chance that the guess is accepted. The token that comes out is x in one of two ways:

1. The draft guesses x (chance q(x)) and it is accepted (chance min(1, p(x)/q(x))): together q(x) · min(1, p(x)/q(x)) = min(q(x), p(x)) = m(x).
2. The guess is rejected (chance 1 − β) and the residual gives x: (1 − β) · max(0, p(x) − q(x)) / Z.

Now Z = Σ max(0, p − q) = Σ (p − m) = 1 − β, because p − m is p − q where p is larger and 0 elsewhere. So the second way is exactly max(0, p(x) − q(x)), and the total is

m(x) + max(0, p(x) − q(x)) = p(x),

since min(p, q) + max(0, p − q) is p whichever of the two is larger. Each kept token is distributed exactly as the target would have sampled it, given everything before it. The draft model changes only the **speed**, never the text's distribution. A bad draft is slow, not wrong.

### Expected speedup

β is the **acceptance rate**: β = Σ min(p, q) = 1 − (total variation distance between p and q). Suppose, to get a formula, each guess is accepted with the same chance α, independently. Then a step yields 1 + α + α² + … + α^k tokens on average (the guess i + 1 counts only if guesses 1 to i were all accepted, and the last term is the bonus or the replacement):

**expected tokens per step = (1 − α^(k+1)) / (1 − α)**

With α = 0.8 and k = 4 that is (1 − 0.8⁵) / 0.2 ≈ 3.36 tokens. If one draft pass costs c = 0.05 of a target pass, the step costs 1 + k · c = 1.2 target passes, so the speedup is about 3.36 / 1.2 ≈ 2.8. [[A longer guess|speedup-curve]] (bigger k) helps only while the extra guesses are likely to survive; the practice finds the best k.

It pays because decode is memory-bound. In a big batch, decode moves towards the compute roof (lesson 1), the k extra positions are no longer free, and the speedup shrinks; servers often turn speculation down under heavy load. And greedy decoding has a simpler version of the rule: keep each guess that equals the target's top token.

**Watch out:**

- **Resampling from p instead of the residual** after a rejection. It looks harmless, but it counts the accepted part twice, and the output leans towards the draft's favourites.
- **Forgetting the bonus token.** The text stays correct, but every fully accepted step wastes the last target position.
- **Using the target's distribution from the wrong position.** Guess i is judged by the target's p computed after the prefix and guesses 1 to i − 1, not after the prefix alone.
- **Dividing by T = 0.** Treat temperature 0 as greedy.

::: context batch-invariance Why the same seed can give different text
Floating-point addition is not associative: (a + b) + c can differ from a + (b + c) in the last bit. Fast kernels choose how to split and order their sums based on the shapes they see, and the batch size is one of those shapes. So a request's logits can change slightly depending on which other requests share its step, and when two tokens are nearly tied, a tiny change flips the pick and the rest of the answer diverges. Making a serving stack batch-invariant, so results do not depend on the batch, takes kernels written to fix the order of every reduction, and costs some speed.
:::

::: context draft-models Where the guesses come from
The draft does not have to be a separate model. Some systems train a few extra prediction heads on the target model itself, each guessing a token further ahead. Others draft from text that is likely to repeat: for code editing or summarising a document, the next tokens often copy a span of the prompt, so matching the last few tokens against the prompt gives free guesses. Any source works, because the acceptance rule keeps the output exact; a better source only raises the acceptance rate.
:::

::: context rejection-sampling An old idea in new clothes
The acceptance rule is a form of rejection sampling, a method from the early days of computing for drawing from a hard distribution p using an easy one q: propose from q, accept with a probability that corrects for the difference. Speculative decoding adds the residual step, which turns every rejection into a usable token instead of a wasted try. The rule was published independently by two research groups in late 2022 and early 2023, and is now standard in production serving engines.
:::

::: context speedup-curve More guesses, diminishing returns
Expected tokens per step for k guesses, with acceptance rates 0.6 and 0.9. At α = 0.6 the curve flattens after 3 or 4 guesses, so extra draft passes are wasted; at 0.9 longer guesses keep paying.

```svg
<svg viewBox="0 0 340 180" xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" font-family="Inter, Arial, sans-serif">
  <line x1="40" y1="150" x2="320" y2="150" stroke="#1f2a44"/>
  <line x1="40" y1="150" x2="40" y2="15" stroke="#1f2a44"/>
  <text x="180" y="172" font-size="11" text-anchor="middle" fill="#1f2a44">guesses k (0 to 8)</text>
  <text x="14" y="85" font-size="11" text-anchor="middle" fill="#1f2a44" transform="rotate(-90 14 85)">tokens per step</text>
  <polyline fill="none" stroke="#1d6fd1" stroke-width="2" points="40,135 75,121.5 110,109.4 145,98.4 180,88.6 215,79.7 250,71.7 285,64.6 320,58.1"/>
  <text x="300" y="48" font-size="11" text-anchor="end" fill="#1d6fd1">α = 0.9: 6.1 at k = 8</text>
  <polyline fill="none" stroke="#b4232c" stroke-width="2" points="40,135 75,126 110,120.6 145,117.4 180,115.4 215,114.3 250,113.6 285,113.1 320,112.9"/>
  <text x="318" y="130" font-size="11" text-anchor="end" fill="#b4232c">α = 0.6: never above 2.5</text>
</svg>
```
:::
--- task
The starter has `sample(dist, rng)`, which draws a token from a dict of probabilities, and a tiny target and draft model: functions that take the tokens so far (a tuple or list) and return a dict `{token: probability}`. Write three functions.

- `residual(p, q)`: the residual distribution as a dict: for each token of `p`, max(0, p(t) − q(t)) divided by the total of those values, leaving out tokens whose value is 0. A token missing from `q` has q(t) = 0. If the total is 0 (p and q agree), return a copy of `p`.
- `speculative_step(target, draft, prefix, k, rng)`: one step of speculative decoding after the tokens `prefix`. Draw k guesses from the draft, one at a time, each after the prefix plus the guesses before it, with `sample(q, rng)`. Then judge them in order: guess x, drawn from q, is accepted when `rng.random() < p.get(x, 0.0) / q[x]`, where p is the target's distribution after the prefix plus the earlier guesses. On the first rejection, append `sample(residual(p, q), rng)` instead and stop. If every guess is accepted, append a bonus `sample(target(...), rng)` after all k guesses. Return the list of new tokens (1 to k + 1 of them). With k = 0 it returns one token from the target.
- `generate(target, draft, k, n, rng)`: call `speculative_step` repeatedly, each time after all tokens so far, until there are at least `n` tokens; return the first `n`.

The checks run thousands of steps and compare the frequencies of what comes out with the target's own probabilities.
--- starter


import random


def sample(dist, rng):
    """Draws one token from a dict {token: probability}, walking the tokens in sorted order."""
    u = rng.random()
    total = 0.0
    last = None
    for token in sorted(dist):
        if dist[token] > 0:
            total += dist[token]
            last = token
            if u < total:
                return token
    return last


# A tiny target model and a cheaper, less accurate draft model: bigram tables,
# so each next-token distribution depends on the last token ("." at the start).
TARGET_TABLE = {
    ".": {"a": 0.5, "b": 0.3, "c": 0.2},
    "a": {"a": 0.1, "b": 0.6, "c": 0.3},
    "b": {"a": 0.7, "b": 0.1, "c": 0.2},
    "c": {"a": 0.3, "b": 0.3, "c": 0.4},
}
DRAFT_TABLE = {
    ".": {"a": 0.3, "b": 0.3, "c": 0.4},
    "a": {"a": 0.2, "b": 0.7, "c": 0.1},
    "b": {"a": 0.4, "b": 0.4, "c": 0.2},
    "c": {"a": 0.6, "b": 0.2, "c": 0.2},
}


def target(prefix):
    return TARGET_TABLE[prefix[-1] if prefix else "."]


def draft(prefix):
    return DRAFT_TABLE[prefix[-1] if prefix else "."]


def residual(p, q):
    return dict(p)


def speculative_step(target, draft, prefix, k, rng):
    guesses = []
    ctx = tuple(prefix)
    for _ in range(k):
        x = sample(draft(ctx), rng)
        guesses.append(x)
        ctx = ctx + (x,)
    return guesses


def generate(target, draft, k, n, rng):
    return []
--- solution


import random


def sample(dist, rng):
    """Draws one token from a dict {token: probability}, walking the tokens in sorted order."""
    u = rng.random()
    total = 0.0
    last = None
    for token in sorted(dist):
        if dist[token] > 0:
            total += dist[token]
            last = token
            if u < total:
                return token
    return last


# A tiny target model and a cheaper, less accurate draft model: bigram tables,
# so each next-token distribution depends on the last token ("." at the start).
TARGET_TABLE = {
    ".": {"a": 0.5, "b": 0.3, "c": 0.2},
    "a": {"a": 0.1, "b": 0.6, "c": 0.3},
    "b": {"a": 0.7, "b": 0.1, "c": 0.2},
    "c": {"a": 0.3, "b": 0.3, "c": 0.4},
}
DRAFT_TABLE = {
    ".": {"a": 0.3, "b": 0.3, "c": 0.4},
    "a": {"a": 0.2, "b": 0.7, "c": 0.1},
    "b": {"a": 0.4, "b": 0.4, "c": 0.2},
    "c": {"a": 0.6, "b": 0.2, "c": 0.2},
}


def target(prefix):
    return TARGET_TABLE[prefix[-1] if prefix else "."]


def draft(prefix):
    return DRAFT_TABLE[prefix[-1] if prefix else "."]


def residual(p, q):
    diff = {t: max(0.0, pt - q.get(t, 0.0)) for t, pt in p.items()}
    total = sum(diff.values())
    if total == 0:
        return dict(p)
    return {t: v / total for t, v in diff.items() if v > 0}


def speculative_step(target, draft, prefix, k, rng):
    prefix = tuple(prefix)
    guesses = []
    ctx = prefix
    for _ in range(k):
        q = draft(ctx)
        x = sample(q, rng)
        guesses.append((x, q))
        ctx = ctx + (x,)
    out = []
    ctx = prefix
    for x, q in guesses:
        p = target(ctx)
        if rng.random() < p.get(x, 0.0) / q[x]:
            out.append(x)
            ctx = ctx + (x,)
        else:
            out.append(sample(residual(p, q), rng))
            return out
    out.append(sample(target(ctx), rng))
    return out


def generate(target, draft, k, n, rng):
    out = []
    while len(out) < n:
        out += speculative_step(target, draft, out, k, rng)
    return out[:n]


--- hint
Draw all k guesses first, remembering each guess together with the draft distribution q it came from. Then walk them again from the prefix, asking the target for p at each position.
--- hint
Accept with `rng.random() < p.get(x, 0.0) / q[x]`; on acceptance extend the context by x. On rejection, append `sample(residual(p, q), rng)` and `return` straight away.
--- hint
If the loop finishes, every guess was accepted: append `sample(target(ctx), rng)` with the context that now includes all k guesses. `residual` builds `max(0.0, p[t] - q.get(t, 0.0))` for every token of p, then divides by the total.
--- check case | The residual keeps only where the target beats the draft
(lambda r: {t: round(v, 9) for t, v in r.items()})(residual({"a": 0.6, "b": 0.3, "c": 0.1}, {"a": 0.2, "b": 0.3, "c": 0.5}))
=> {"a": 1.0}
--- check case | A residual over two tokens, and a token the draft never proposes
(lambda r: {t: round(v, 9) for t, v in r.items()})(residual({"a": 0.5, "b": 0.3, "c": 0.2}, {"a": 0.3, "c": 0.7}))
=> {"a": 0.4, "b": 0.6}
--- check case | When the two agree, the residual is the target itself
residual({"x": 0.25, "y": 0.75}, {"x": 0.25, "y": 0.75})
=> {"x": 0.25, "y": 0.75}

--- check test | k = 0 is plain sampling from the target

--- check test | A perfect draft is always accepted: k + 1 tokens every step

--- check test | The first token's frequencies match the target's (20,000 steps)

?? If "a" comes out too rarely or too often, check what you sample after a rejection: it must be the residual, not p.
--- check test | Pairs of tokens match the target's joint probabilities (20,000 runs of generate)
(lambda rng: (lambda c: all(abs(c[(x, y)] / 20000 - TARGET_TABLE["."][x] * TARGET_TABLE[x][y]) < 0.015 for x in "abc" for y in "abc"))(__import__("collections").Counter(tuple(generate(target, draft, 3, 2, rng)) for _ in range(20000))))(__import__("random").Random(12))
?? The second token is judged by the target's distribution after the first token, not after the empty prefix.

?? About 2.31 tokens per step. Fewer means a missing bonus token; more means guesses are kept too easily.
--- check test | A draft that never agrees still gives exactly the target's distribution, one token per step

--- check case | generate returns exactly n tokens
(lambda rng: [len(generate(target, draft, 4, n, rng)) for n in (1, 2, 7, 30)])(__import__("random").Random(15))
=> [1, 2, 7, 30]

+++ question | What the residual is for
--- ask
After a rejected guess, speculative decoding draws the replacement from max(0, p − q), rescaled, instead of from the target's p. Why?
--- choice
Because sampling from p again would be too slow.
--- choice correct
Because the accepted guesses already supply min(p, q) of each token's probability; the residual supplies exactly the missing part, p − min(p, q), so the total is p.
--- choice
Because the residual makes the replacement more likely to match the next draft guess.
--- choice
Because the target's p is not available after a rejection.
--- why
Accepting x with probability min(1, p/q) gives x a chance of min(p(x), q(x)). The rejection branch must add max(0, p(x) − q(x)) for the total to be p(x); the rescaled residual times the rejection chance 1 − β is exactly that. Drawing from p would count the min(p, q) part twice.

+++ question | Tokens per step
--- ask
A draft model's guesses are each accepted with probability 0.5, independently. With k = 2 guesses per step, how many tokens does a step produce on average? Type a decimal number.
--- answer
1.75
7/4
--- why
(1 − α^(k+1)) / (1 − α) = (1 − 0.125) / 0.5 = 1.75. Or term by term: 1 token always (the first guess or its replacement), plus 0.5 for the second, plus 0.25 for the bonus.

+++ question | When speculation stops paying
--- ask
A server runs speculative decoding with k = 4. Under light load it doubles the speed of each stream. Under heavy load, with batches of several hundred requests, the gain almost vanishes. Why?
--- choice
The draft model's acceptance rate drops when many requests share a step.
--- choice correct
Big batches push decode towards the compute roof, so checking 5 positions per request is no longer nearly free: the target pass gets about 5 times as expensive.
--- choice
The acceptance rule is only exact for batch size 1, so servers must turn it off.
--- choice
The KV cache cannot hold the guessed tokens of so many requests.
--- why
Speculation spends idle compute. At batch 1 the target pass is memory-bound and 5 positions cost about as much as 1. At a batch near the ridge point, compute is no longer idle, so every extra position costs real time, and the speedup formula's cost side grows.

+++ practice | Temperature, including zero
--- task
Write `with_temperature(logits, t)` that turns a list of logits into a list of probabilities, each rounded to 6 decimal places:

- for t > 0: the softmax of `logits[i] / t`. Subtract the largest scaled logit before calling `math.exp`, so huge logits do not overflow (the stable softmax from the Python AI course);
- for t = 0: greedy, a list with 1.0 at the position of the largest logit (the first one, if several tie) and 0.0 everywhere else.

For example, `with_temperature([2.0, 1.0, 0.0], 1.0)` is `[0.665241, 0.244728, 0.090031]`.
--- starter
import math


def with_temperature(logits, t):
    exps = [math.exp(x / t) for x in logits]
    return [round(e / sum(exps), 6) for e in exps]
--- solution
import math


def with_temperature(logits, t):
    if t == 0:
        best = logits.index(max(logits))
        return [1.0 if i == best else 0.0 for i in range(len(logits))]
    scaled = [x / t for x in logits]
    top = max(scaled)
    exps = [math.exp(x - top) for x in scaled]
    total = sum(exps)
    return [round(e / total, 6) for e in exps]
--- hint
Handle t = 0 first: `logits.index(max(logits))` finds the first position of the largest logit.
--- hint
Otherwise scale every logit by 1/t, subtract the largest scaled value, take `math.exp`, and divide by the total.
--- check case | The example from the task
with_temperature([2.0, 1.0, 0.0], 1.0)
=> [0.665241, 0.244728, 0.090031]
--- check case | A low temperature sharpens, a high one flattens
(with_temperature([2.0, 1.0, 0.0], 0.5), with_temperature([2.0, 1.0, 0.0], 4.0))
=> ([0.866813, 0.11731, 0.015876], [0.419229, 0.326496, 0.254275])
--- check case | Temperature 0 is greedy, and ties go to the first
(with_temperature([0.5, 3.0, -1.0], 0), with_temperature([4.0, 1.0, 4.0], 0))
=> ([0.0, 1.0, 0.0], [1.0, 0.0, 0.0])
--- check case | Huge logits do not overflow
with_temperature([1000.0, 999.0], 1.0)
=> [0.731059, 0.268941]
--- check case | Equal logits give equal probabilities at any temperature
with_temperature([3.0, 3.0, 3.0, 3.0], 0.1)
=> [0.25, 0.25, 0.25, 0.25]

+++ practice | Top-k, then top-p
--- task
Write `truncate(probs, k, p)`. `probs` is a list of probabilities (adding up to 1) indexed by token id. Keep:

1. the `k` most likely tokens (all of them if `k` is `None`);
2. of those, the smallest set of the most likely whose probabilities add up to at least `p`, taking tokens from most to least likely;

then rescale the kept probabilities so they add up to 1. Return a dict `{token id: probability}` with each probability rounded to 6 decimal places. Order the tokens from most to least likely, breaking ties by the smaller id. Compare the running total with `p` after adding each token, and stop as soon as it reaches `p`; to guard against float rounding, treat a total within 1e-9 of `p` as reaching it.

For example, `truncate([0.1, 0.5, 0.15, 0.25], None, 0.7)` is `{1: 0.666667, 3: 0.333333}`: 0.5 alone is short of 0.7, and 0.5 + 0.25 reaches it.
--- starter
def truncate(probs, k, p):
    return {i: round(x, 6) for i, x in enumerate(probs)}
--- solution
def truncate(probs, k, p):
    order = sorted(range(len(probs)), key=lambda i: (-probs[i], i))
    if k is not None:
        order = order[:k]
    kept, total = [], 0.0
    for i in order:
        kept.append(i)
        total += probs[i]
        if total >= p - 1e-9:
            break
    mass = sum(probs[i] for i in kept)
    return {i: round(probs[i] / mass, 6) for i in kept}
--- hint
Sort the token ids by probability, highest first, with ties to the smaller id: `sorted(range(len(probs)), key=lambda i: (-probs[i], i))`. Cut that list to k first.
--- hint
Walk the sorted ids adding probabilities, and stop right after the one that brings the total to `p` (allowing 1e-9 of rounding). Rescale by the sum of what you kept.
--- check case | The example from the task
truncate([0.1, 0.5, 0.15, 0.25], None, 0.7)
=> {1: 0.666667, 3: 0.333333}
--- check case | Top-k alone (p = 1.0)
truncate([0.1, 0.5, 0.15, 0.25], 2, 1.0)
=> {1: 0.666667, 3: 0.333333}
--- check case | Top-k first, then top-p inside it
truncate([0.05, 0.4, 0.3, 0.15, 0.1], 3, 0.5)
=> {1: 0.571429, 2: 0.428571}
--- check case | A confident model keeps one token
truncate([0.02, 0.95, 0.03], None, 0.9)
=> {1: 1.0}
--- check case | Landing exactly on p stops there, despite float rounding
truncate([0.1, 0.2, 0.3, 0.4], None, 0.7)
=> {3: 0.571429, 2: 0.428571}
--- check case | Ties go to the smaller id
truncate([0.25, 0.25, 0.25, 0.25], 2, 1.0)
=> {0: 0.5, 1: 0.5}

+++ practice | The best number of guesses
--- task
Use the lesson's model of speculative decoding: each guess is accepted with probability `alpha`, independently, and a draft pass costs `c` target passes.

- `expected_tokens(alpha, k)`: the expected tokens per step, (1 − alpha^(k+1)) / (1 − alpha). When `alpha` is 1, every guess is accepted: return `k + 1`.
- `speedup(alpha, k, c)`: expected tokens divided by the step's cost, 1 + k · c (in target passes).
- `best_k(alpha, c, k_max)`: the k from 0 to `k_max` with the largest speedup; on a tie, the smaller k. Return the pair `(k, speedup)` with the speedup rounded to 4 decimal places.

For example, `expected_tokens(0.8, 4)` is about 3.3616, and `best_k(0.8, 0.05, 10)` is `(8, 3.0921)`.
--- starter
def expected_tokens(alpha, k):
    return 1.0


def speedup(alpha, k, c):
    return 1.0


def best_k(alpha, c, k_max):
    return (0, 1.0)
--- solution
def expected_tokens(alpha, k):
    if alpha == 1:
        return k + 1
    return (1 - alpha ** (k + 1)) / (1 - alpha)


def speedup(alpha, k, c):
    return expected_tokens(alpha, k) / (1 + k * c)


def best_k(alpha, c, k_max):
    best = max(range(k_max + 1), key=lambda k: (speedup(alpha, k, c), -k))
    return (best, round(speedup(alpha, best, c), 4))
--- hint
`expected_tokens` is the formula, with the special case `alpha == 1` first (the formula would divide by zero).
--- hint
`best_k` tries every k from 0 to `k_max`. With `max(..., key=lambda k: (speedup(...), -k))`, a tie in speedup is broken by the larger `-k`, which is the smaller k.
--- check test | expected_tokens for the lesson's example, and at the ends
abs(expected_tokens(0.8, 4) - 3.36160) < 1e-9 and expected_tokens(0.3, 0) == 1 and expected_tokens(1, 6) == 7 and expected_tokens(0.0, 5) == 1
--- check case | best_k for the example
best_k(0.8, 0.05, 10)
=> (8, 3.0921)
--- check case | A weak draft: guessing does not pay at all
best_k(0.1, 0.2, 8)
=> (0, 1.0)
--- check case | A perfect, free draft: use every guess allowed
best_k(1, 0.0, 6)
=> (6, 7.0)
--- check case | A good draft but an expensive one
best_k(0.9, 0.25, 12)
=> (6, 2.0868)
--- check case | speedup itself
round(speedup(0.7, 3, 0.1), 4)
=> 1.9485

+++ practice | Pricing a speculative step with the cost model
--- task
The starter has lesson 1's cost model. A speculative step for one request with `ctx` tokens in its cache runs the draft model `k` times, one decode each (`[(1, ctx + i)]` for i from 0 to k − 1, on the draft model), then the target model once over k + 1 positions (`[(k + 1, ctx)]`: the last real token plus the k guesses).

Write `spec_rate(target_model, draft_model, hw, ctx, k, alpha)` that returns a tuple `(step_time, plain_rate, spec_rate)`:

- `step_time`: the time of one speculative step (the k draft steps plus the target step), rounded to 9 decimal places;
- `plain_rate`: tokens per second of ordinary decoding, 1 / the time of `[(1, ctx)]` on the target, rounded to 1 decimal place;
- `spec_rate`: expected tokens per step, (1 − alpha^(k+1)) / (1 − alpha) (or k + 1 when alpha is 1), divided by the unrounded step time, rounded to 1 decimal place.

For k = 0 the step is one target step over one position, and `spec_rate` equals `plain_rate`.
--- starter


import math
import random


class Model:
    """The shapes of a decoder-only transformer that set what serving it costs."""

    def __init__(self, n_params, n_layers, d_model, n_kv_heads, head_dim, bytes_per_param=2, kv_bytes=2):
        self.n_params = n_params
        self.n_layers = n_layers
        self.d_model = d_model
        self.n_kv_heads = n_kv_heads
        self.head_dim = head_dim
        self.bytes_per_param = bytes_per_param
        self.kv_bytes = kv_bytes


class Accel:
    """One accelerator: peak FLOP/s, memory bandwidth in bytes/s, memory size in bytes."""

    def __init__(self, peak_flops, mem_bw, mem_bytes):
        self.peak_flops = peak_flops
        self.mem_bw = mem_bw
        self.mem_bytes = mem_bytes


def kv_bytes_per_token(model):
    return 2 * model.n_layers * model.n_kv_heads * model.head_dim * model.kv_bytes


def step_cost(model, hw, work):
    if not work:
        return {"flops": 0, "bytes": 0, "time": 0.0, "bound": "idle"}
    tokens = sum(n for n, _ in work)
    keys = sum(n * c + n * (n + 1) // 2 for n, c in work)
    flops = 2 * model.n_params * tokens + 4 * model.n_layers * model.d_model * keys
    kv = kv_bytes_per_token(model)
    moved = model.n_params * model.bytes_per_param + kv * sum(c + n for n, c in work)
    t_compute = flops / hw.peak_flops
    t_memory = moved / hw.mem_bw
    bound = "compute" if t_compute > t_memory else "memory"
    return {"flops": flops, "bytes": moved, "time": max(t_compute, t_memory), "bound": bound}


def spec_rate(target_model, draft_model, hw, ctx, k, alpha):
    return (0.0, 0.0, 0.0)
--- solution


import math
import random


class Model:
    """The shapes of a decoder-only transformer that set what serving it costs."""

    def __init__(self, n_params, n_layers, d_model, n_kv_heads, head_dim, bytes_per_param=2, kv_bytes=2):
        self.n_params = n_params
        self.n_layers = n_layers
        self.d_model = d_model
        self.n_kv_heads = n_kv_heads
        self.head_dim = head_dim
        self.bytes_per_param = bytes_per_param
        self.kv_bytes = kv_bytes


class Accel:
    """One accelerator: peak FLOP/s, memory bandwidth in bytes/s, memory size in bytes."""

    def __init__(self, peak_flops, mem_bw, mem_bytes):
        self.peak_flops = peak_flops
        self.mem_bw = mem_bw
        self.mem_bytes = mem_bytes


def kv_bytes_per_token(model):
    return 2 * model.n_layers * model.n_kv_heads * model.head_dim * model.kv_bytes


def step_cost(model, hw, work):
    if not work:
        return {"flops": 0, "bytes": 0, "time": 0.0, "bound": "idle"}
    tokens = sum(n for n, _ in work)
    keys = sum(n * c + n * (n + 1) // 2 for n, c in work)
    flops = 2 * model.n_params * tokens + 4 * model.n_layers * model.d_model * keys
    kv = kv_bytes_per_token(model)
    moved = model.n_params * model.bytes_per_param + kv * sum(c + n for n, c in work)
    t_compute = flops / hw.peak_flops
    t_memory = moved / hw.mem_bw
    bound = "compute" if t_compute > t_memory else "memory"
    return {"flops": flops, "bytes": moved, "time": max(t_compute, t_memory), "bound": bound}


def spec_rate(target_model, draft_model, hw, ctx, k, alpha):
    t = sum(step_cost(draft_model, hw, [(1, ctx + i)])["time"] for i in range(k))
    t += step_cost(target_model, hw, [(k + 1, ctx)])["time"]
    plain = 1 / step_cost(target_model, hw, [(1, ctx)])["time"]
    tokens = k + 1 if alpha == 1 else (1 - alpha ** (k + 1)) / (1 - alpha)
    return (round(t, 9), round(plain, 1), round(tokens / t, 1))
--- hint
Add up k calls of `step_cost` on the draft model, each one token further into the context, and one call on the target model with `[(k + 1, ctx)]`.
--- hint
Verifying k + 1 positions on the target costs almost the same as one decode, because the step is memory-bound: that is where the speedup comes from.
--- check case | A 7B target with a 0.5B draft, 4 guesses, 80% accepted
spec_rate(Model(7_000_000_000, 32, 4096, 8, 128), Model(500_000_000, 24, 1024, 2, 64), Accel(1e15, 3.35e12, 80e9), 1000, 4, 0.8)
=> (0.005427165, 237.1, 619.4)
--- check case | k = 0 is ordinary decoding
(lambda r: r[1] == r[2])(spec_rate(Model(7_000_000_000, 32, 4096, 8, 128), Model(500_000_000, 24, 1024, 2, 64), Accel(1e15, 3.35e12, 80e9), 1000, 0, 0.8))
=> True
--- check case | A perfect draft with 6 guesses
spec_rate(Model(7_000_000_000, 32, 4096, 8, 128), Model(500_000_000, 24, 1024, 2, 64), Accel(1e15, 3.35e12, 80e9), 500, 6, 1)
=> (0.006001067, 238.2, 1166.5)
--- check test | Checking 5 positions costs less than 1.1 ordinary decode steps
(lambda m, hw: step_cost(m, hw, [(5, 1000)])["time"] < 1.1 * step_cost(m, hw, [(1, 1000)])["time"])(Model(7_000_000_000, 32, 4096, 8, 128), Accel(1e15, 3.35e12, 80e9))

+++ practice | Debug: text that leans towards the draft
--- task
