class Node:
    def __init__(self, tokens, parent):
        self.tokens = tokens
        self.parent = parent
        self.children = {}
        self.last_used = 0


def shared(a, b):
    n = 0
    while n < len(a) and n < len(b) and a[n] == b[n]:
        n += 1
    return n


class RadixCache:
    """Cached token prefixes in a radix tree, at most `capacity` tokens, evicting least recently used leaves."""

    def __init__(self, capacity):
        self.capacity = capacity
        self.root = Node((), None)
        self.total = 0

    def size(self):
        return self.total

    def match(self, tokens, now=None):
        tokens = tuple(tokens)
        node, i = self.root, 0
        while i < len(tokens) and tokens[i] in node.children:
            child = node.children[tokens[i]]
            n = shared(child.tokens, tokens[i:])
            if now is not None:
                child.last_used = now
            i += n
            if n < len(child.tokens):
                break
            node = child
        return i

    def insert(self, tokens, now=0):
        tokens = tuple(tokens)
        node, i = self.root, 0
        while i < len(tokens):
            child = node.children.get(tokens[i])
            if child is None:
                leaf = Node(tokens[i:], node)
                leaf.last_used = now
                node.children[tokens[i]] = leaf
                self.total += len(tokens) - i
                break
            n = shared(child.tokens, tokens[i:])
            if n < len(child.tokens):
                mid = Node(child.tokens[:n], node)
                node.children[tokens[i]] = mid
                child.tokens = child.tokens[n:]
                child.parent = mid
                mid.children[child.tokens[0]] = child
                child = mid
            child.last_used = now
            node = child
            i += n
        self.evict()

    def path(self, node):
        parts = []
        while node is not self.root:
            parts.append(node.tokens)
            node = node.parent
        return tuple(t for part in reversed(parts) for t in part)

    def evict(self):
        while self.total > self.capacity:
            leaves, stack = [], [self.root]
            while stack:
                node = stack.pop()
                stack.extend(node.children.values())
                if not node.children and node is not self.root:
                    leaves.append(node)
            victim = min(leaves, key=lambda n: (n.last_used, self.path(n)))
            del victim.parent.children[victim.tokens[0]]
            self.total -= len(victim.tokens)


import random


def make_prompts(seed, n, n_system=4, system_len=200, user_len=(20, 80), vocab=1000, weights=None):
    """n chat prompts: one of n_system shared system prompts (chosen with the given weights,
    or uniformly), then the user's own tokens."""
    rng = random.Random(seed)
    systems = [[rng.randrange(vocab) for _ in range(system_len)] for _ in range(n_system)]
    out = []
    for _ in range(n):
        user = [rng.randrange(vocab) for _ in range(rng.randint(*user_len))]
        pick = rng.randrange(n_system) if weights is None else rng.choices(range(n_system), weights)[0]
        out.append(systems[pick] + user)
    return out


def sharing(prompts):
    total = sum(len(p) for p in prompts)
    cache = RadixCache(total)
    shared = []
    for p in prompts:
        shared.append(cache.match(p))
        cache.insert(p)
    return (shared, round(sum(shared) / total, 4) if total else 0.0)
--- hint
Match each prompt against everything inserted before it, then insert it. A capacity equal to the total number of tokens never evicts anything.
--- hint
Watch the empty case: with no tokens, return a rate of 0.0 instead of dividing by zero.
--- check case | The example from the task
sharing([[1, 2, 3], [1, 2, 4, 5], [1, 9], [1, 2, 4]])
=> ([0, 2, 1, 3], 0.5)
--- check case | A repeated prompt is shared in full
sharing([[4, 4, 4], [4, 4, 4]])
=> ([0, 3], 0.5)
--- check case | Nothing in common
sharing([[1], [2], [3, 4]])
=> ([0, 0, 0], 0.0)
--- check case | No prompts
sharing([])
=> ([], 0.0)
--- check case | 200 chat prompts with 4 system prompts of 200 tokens
(lambda r: (r[0][:6], r[1]))(sharing(make_prompts(2, 200)))
=> ([0, 0, 200, 200, 0, 200], 0.7829)

+++ practice | Prefill with a cached prefix
--- task
The starter has lesson 1's cost model. Write `cached_prefill(model, hw, prompt_len, cached)` that returns `(without, with_cache)`: the time of the prefill step for a prompt of `prompt_len` tokens with no cache (`[(prompt_len, 0)]`), and with the first `cached` tokens already in the cache, each rounded to 9 decimal places.

With the cache, the step processes the tokens that are not cached, attending to the cached ones: `[(prompt_len − cached, cached)]`. But a step always processes **at least one** token, because the first output token needs the last position's logits: if the whole prompt is cached (or `cached` is even larger, because the cached prefix runs past this prompt), the step is `[(1, prompt_len − 1)]`. `prompt_len` is at least 1 and `cached` at least 0.
--- starter


import math
import random


class Model:
    """The shapes of a decoder-only transformer that set what serving it costs."""

    def __init__(self, n_params, n_layers, d_model, n_kv_heads, head_dim, bytes_per_param=2, kv_bytes=2):
        self.n_params = n_params
        self.n_layers = n_layers
        self.d_model = d_model
        self.n_kv_heads = n_kv_heads
        self.head_dim = head_dim
        self.bytes_per_param = bytes_per_param
        self.kv_bytes = kv_bytes


class Accel:
    """One accelerator: peak FLOP/s, memory bandwidth in bytes/s, memory size in bytes."""

    def __init__(self, peak_flops, mem_bw, mem_bytes):
        self.peak_flops = peak_flops
        self.mem_bw = mem_bw
        self.mem_bytes = mem_bytes


def kv_bytes_per_token(model):
    return 2 * model.n_layers * model.n_kv_heads * model.head_dim * model.kv_bytes


def step_cost(model, hw, work):
    if not work:
        return {"flops": 0, "bytes": 0, "time": 0.0, "bound": "idle"}
    tokens = sum(n for n, _ in work)
    keys = sum(n * c + n * (n + 1) // 2 for n, c in work)
    flops = 2 * model.n_params * tokens + 4 * model.n_layers * model.d_model * keys
    kv = kv_bytes_per_token(model)
    moved = model.n_params * model.bytes_per_param + kv * sum(c + n for n, c in work)
    t_compute = flops / hw.peak_flops
    t_memory = moved / hw.mem_bw
    bound = "compute" if t_compute > t_memory else "memory"
    return {"flops": flops, "bytes": moved, "time": max(t_compute, t_memory), "bound": bound}


def cached_prefill(model, hw, prompt_len, cached):
    without = step_cost(model, hw, [(prompt_len, 0)])["time"]
    with_cache = step_cost(model, hw, [(prompt_len - cached, cached)])["time"]
    return (round(without, 9), round(with_cache, 9))
--- solution


import math
import random


class Model:
    """The shapes of a decoder-only transformer that set what serving it costs."""

    def __init__(self, n_params, n_layers, d_model, n_kv_heads, head_dim, bytes_per_param=2, kv_bytes=2):
        self.n_params = n_params
        self.n_layers = n_layers
        self.d_model = d_model
        self.n_kv_heads = n_kv_heads
        self.head_dim = head_dim
        self.bytes_per_param = bytes_per_param
        self.kv_bytes = kv_bytes


class Accel:
    """One accelerator: peak FLOP/s, memory bandwidth in bytes/s, memory size in bytes."""

    def __init__(self, peak_flops, mem_bw, mem_bytes):
        self.peak_flops = peak_flops
        self.mem_bw = mem_bw
        self.mem_bytes = mem_bytes


def kv_bytes_per_token(model):
    return 2 * model.n_layers * model.n_kv_heads * model.head_dim * model.kv_bytes


def step_cost(model, hw, work):
    if not work:
        return {"flops": 0, "bytes": 0, "time": 0.0, "bound": "idle"}
    tokens = sum(n for n, _ in work)
    keys = sum(n * c + n * (n + 1) // 2 for n, c in work)
    flops = 2 * model.n_params * tokens + 4 * model.n_layers * model.d_model * keys
    kv = kv_bytes_per_token(model)
    moved = model.n_params * model.bytes_per_param + kv * sum(c + n for n, c in work)
    t_compute = flops / hw.peak_flops
    t_memory = moved / hw.mem_bw
    bound = "compute" if t_compute > t_memory else "memory"
    return {"flops": flops, "bytes": moved, "time": max(t_compute, t_memory), "bound": bound}


def cached_prefill(model, hw, prompt_len, cached):
    without = step_cost(model, hw, [(prompt_len, 0)])["time"]
    hit = min(cached, prompt_len - 1)
    with_cache = step_cost(model, hw, [(prompt_len - hit, hit)])["time"]
    return (round(without, 9), round(with_cache, 9))
--- hint
Cap the usable hit at `prompt_len - 1`, so at least one token is always processed.
--- hint
With the capped hit h, the work is `[(prompt_len - h, h)]`. That also covers a cache hit longer than the prompt.
--- check case | 1,000 tokens with 800 cached
cached_prefill(Model(7_000_000_000, 32, 4096, 8, 128), Accel(1e15, 3.35e12, 80e9), 1000, 800)
=> (0.014262406, 0.00421823)
--- check case | Nothing cached
cached_prefill(Model(7_000_000_000, 32, 4096, 8, 128), Accel(1e15, 3.35e12, 80e9), 1000, 0)
=> (0.014262406, 0.014262406)
--- check case | The whole prompt cached: one token still runs
cached_prefill(Model(7_000_000_000, 32, 4096, 8, 128), Accel(1e15, 3.35e12, 80e9), 1000, 1000)
=> (0.014262406, 0.00421823)
--- check case | A cached prefix longer than the prompt counts as fully cached
(lambda a, b: a == b)(cached_prefill(Model(7_000_000_000, 32, 4096, 8, 128), Accel(1e15, 3.35e12, 80e9), 600, 5000), cached_prefill(Model(7_000_000_000, 32, 4096, 8, 128), Accel(1e15, 3.35e12, 80e9), 600, 600))
=> True
--- check case | On a compute-starved accelerator the one remaining token is what costs
cached_prefill(Model(7_000_000_000, 32, 4096, 8, 128), Accel(1e12, 1e15, 80e9), 1000, 1000)
=> (14.262406144, 0.014524288)
--- check case | And a prefix longer than the prompt costs the same one token
cached_prefill(Model(7_000_000_000, 32, 4096, 8, 128), Accel(1e12, 1e15, 80e9), 1000, 4000)
=> (14.262406144, 0.014524288)
--- check case | A one-token prompt
cached_prefill(Model(7_000_000_000, 32, 4096, 8, 128), Accel(1e15, 3.35e12, 80e9), 1, 1)
=> (0.004179144, 0.004179144)

+++ practice | Routing on the cache, on load, or both
--- task
A router sends each prompt, in order, to one of `n` replicas. Each replica has its own `RadixCache(capacity)` (from the starter) and a load: the total number of **uncached** prompt tokens it has been sent so far. Write `route(prompts, n, capacity, alpha)` that, for prompt number t (counting from 0):

1. for each replica r, finds `m[r]`, how long a prefix of the prompt its cache holds (look without refreshing times: `match(prompt)` with no `now`);
2. picks the replica with the highest score `m[r] − alpha * load[r]` (the smallest index on a tie);
3. on the chosen replica only, calls `match(prompt, t)` and then `insert(prompt, t)`, and adds `len(prompt) − m[r]` to its load.

Return a tuple `(choices, hits, loads)`: the list of chosen replicas, the total of the chosen `m[r]` (tokens served from cache), and the final list of loads.

With `alpha` large, this is least-loaded routing; with `alpha` 0, it chases cache hits. The checks compare the two on chat prompts that share system prompts.
--- starter


class Node:
    def __init__(self, tokens, parent):
        self.tokens = tokens
        self.parent = parent
        self.children = {}
        self.last_used = 0


def shared(a, b):
    n = 0
    while n < len(a) and n < len(b) and a[n] == b[n]:
        n += 1
    return n


class RadixCache:
    """Cached token prefixes in a radix tree, at most `capacity` tokens, evicting least recently used leaves."""

    def __init__(self, capacity):
        self.capacity = capacity
        self.root = Node((), None)
        self.total = 0

    def size(self):
        return self.total

    def match(self, tokens, now=None):
        tokens = tuple(tokens)
        node, i = self.root, 0
        while i < len(tokens) and tokens[i] in node.children:
            child = node.children[tokens[i]]
            n = shared(child.tokens, tokens[i:])
            if now is not None:
                child.last_used = now
            i += n
            if n < len(child.tokens):
                break
            node = child
        return i

    def insert(self, tokens, now=0):
        tokens = tuple(tokens)
        node, i = self.root, 0
        while i < len(tokens):
            child = node.children.get(tokens[i])
            if child is None:
                leaf = Node(tokens[i:], node)
                leaf.last_used = now
                node.children[tokens[i]] = leaf
                self.total += len(tokens) - i
                break
            n = shared(child.tokens, tokens[i:])
            if n < len(child.tokens):
                mid = Node(child.tokens[:n], node)
                node.children[tokens[i]] = mid
                child.tokens = child.tokens[n:]
                child.parent = mid
                mid.children[child.tokens[0]] = child
                child = mid
            child.last_used = now
            node = child
            i += n
        self.evict()

    def path(self, node):
        parts = []
        while node is not self.root:
            parts.append(node.tokens)
            node = node.parent
        return tuple(t for part in reversed(parts) for t in part)

    def evict(self):
        while self.total > self.capacity:
            leaves, stack = [], [self.root]
            while stack:
                node = stack.pop()
                stack.extend(node.children.values())
                if not node.children and node is not self.root:
                    leaves.append(node)
            victim = min(leaves, key=lambda n: (n.last_used, self.path(n)))
            del victim.parent.children[victim.tokens[0]]
            self.total -= len(victim.tokens)


import random


def make_prompts(seed, n, n_system=4, system_len=200, user_len=(20, 80), vocab=1000, weights=None):
    """n chat prompts: one of n_system shared system prompts (chosen with the given weights,
    or uniformly), then the user's own tokens."""
    rng = random.Random(seed)
    systems = [[rng.randrange(vocab) for _ in range(system_len)] for _ in range(n_system)]
    out = []
    for _ in range(n):
        user = [rng.randrange(vocab) for _ in range(rng.randint(*user_len))]
        pick = rng.randrange(n_system) if weights is None else rng.choices(range(n_system), weights)[0]
        out.append(systems[pick] + user)
    return out


def route(prompts, n, capacity, alpha):
    loads = [0] * n
    choices = []
    for t, p in enumerate(prompts):
        r = t % n
        choices.append(r)
        loads[r] += len(p)
    return (choices, 0, loads)
--- solution


class Node:
    def __init__(self, tokens, parent):
        self.tokens = tokens
        self.parent = parent
        self.children = {}
        self.last_used = 0


def shared(a, b):
    n = 0
    while n < len(a) and n < len(b) and a[n] == b[n]:
        n += 1
    return n


class RadixCache:
    """Cached token prefixes in a radix tree, at most `capacity` tokens, evicting least recently used leaves."""

    def __init__(self, capacity):
        self.capacity = capacity
        self.root = Node((), None)
        self.total = 0

    def size(self):
        return self.total

    def match(self, tokens, now=None):
        tokens = tuple(tokens)
        node, i = self.root, 0
        while i < len(tokens) and tokens[i] in node.children:
            child = node.children[tokens[i]]
            n = shared(child.tokens, tokens[i:])
            if now is not None:
                child.last_used = now
            i += n
            if n < len(child.tokens):
                break
            node = child
        return i

    def insert(self, tokens, now=0):
        tokens = tuple(tokens)
        node, i = self.root, 0
        while i < len(tokens):
            child = node.children.get(tokens[i])
            if child is None:
                leaf = Node(tokens[i:], node)
                leaf.last_used = now
                node.children[tokens[i]] = leaf
                self.total += len(tokens) - i
                break
            n = shared(child.tokens, tokens[i:])
            if n < len(child.tokens):
                mid = Node(child.tokens[:n], node)
                node.children[tokens[i]] = mid
                child.tokens = child.tokens[n:]
                child.parent = mid
                mid.children[child.tokens[0]] = child
                child = mid
            child.last_used = now
            node = child
            i += n
        self.evict()

    def path(self, node):
        parts = []
        while node is not self.root:
            parts.append(node.tokens)
            node = node.parent
        return tuple(t for part in reversed(parts) for t in part)

    def evict(self):
        while self.total > self.capacity:
            leaves, stack = [], [self.root]
            while stack:
                node = stack.pop()
                stack.extend(node.children.values())
                if not node.children and node is not self.root:
                    leaves.append(node)
            victim = min(leaves, key=lambda n: (n.last_used, self.path(n)))
            del victim.parent.children[victim.tokens[0]]
            self.total -= len(victim.tokens)


import random


def make_prompts(seed, n, n_system=4, system_len=200, user_len=(20, 80), vocab=1000, weights=None):
    """n chat prompts: one of n_system shared system prompts (chosen with the given weights,
    or uniformly), then the user's own tokens."""
    rng = random.Random(seed)
    systems = [[rng.randrange(vocab) for _ in range(system_len)] for _ in range(n_system)]
    out = []
    for _ in range(n):
        user = [rng.randrange(vocab) for _ in range(rng.randint(*user_len))]
        pick = rng.randrange(n_system) if weights is None else rng.choices(range(n_system), weights)[0]
        out.append(systems[pick] + user)
    return out


def route(prompts, n, capacity, alpha):
    caches = [RadixCache(capacity) for _ in range(n)]
    loads = [0] * n
    choices, hits = [], 0
    for t, p in enumerate(prompts):
        m = [c.match(p) for c in caches]
        r = max(range(n), key=lambda i: (m[i] - alpha * loads[i], -i))
        caches[r].match(p, t)
        caches[r].insert(p, t)
        loads[r] += len(p) - m[r]
        hits += m[r]
        choices.append(r)
    return (choices, hits, loads)
--- hint
Keep one `RadixCache` and one load counter per replica. Score every replica with a match that passes no time, so scoring does not change any cache.
--- hint
`max(range(n), key=lambda i: (m[i] - alpha * loads[i], -i))` picks the highest score with ties to the smaller index. Only the chosen replica's cache is touched and inserted into.
--- check case | Three prompts, two replicas, cache-chasing
route([[1, 2, 3], [1, 2, 4], [7, 7]], 2, 100, 0)
=> ([0, 0, 0], 2, [6, 0])
--- check case | The same prompts with a strong load term
route([[1, 2, 3], [1, 2, 4], [7, 7]], 2, 100, 10)
=> ([0, 1, 0], 0, [5, 3])
--- check case | Chat prompts on 4 replicas: hits and loads for cache-chasing, mixed and least-loaded routing
[(lambda r: (r[1], max(r[2]) - min(r[2])))(route(make_prompts(3, 400, n_system=8), 4, 2000, a)) for a in (0, 0.05, 1000)]
=> [(62800, 36148), (78405, 1342), (62400, 122)]
--- check test | Least-loaded routing balances the load, cache-chasing wins the hits
(lambda a, b: a[1] > b[1] and max(b[2]) - min(b[2]) < max(a[2]) - min(a[2]))(route(make_prompts(3, 400, n_system=8), 4, 2000, 0), route(make_prompts(3, 400, n_system=8), 4, 2000, 1000))
--- check test | Every prompt token is either a hit or load on some replica
(lambda ps: (lambda r: r[1] + sum(r[2]) == sum(len(p) for p in ps) and len(r[0]) == len(ps))(route(ps, 3, 1500, 0.02)))(make_prompts(5, 300, n_system=6))

+++ practice | Session affinity on a hash ring
--- task
Send every turn of a conversation to the same replica with **consistent hashing**, as in Distributed Systems I. Use this hash: `h(s) = int.from_bytes(hashlib.sha256(s.encode()).digest()[:8], "big")`, a whole number from a string.

Write `ring_route(sessions, replicas, vnodes)`. Each replica name r gets `vnodes` points on the ring, at h(f"{r}#{i}") for i from 0 to `vnodes − 1`. A session id s goes to the replica owning the first point at or after h(s), wrapping round to the smallest point if there is none. Return a dict from each session id to its replica. If `replicas` is empty, return an empty dict.

The checks confirm that removing one replica moves only the sessions that were on it, and that the load spreads out.
--- starter
import hashlib


def ring_route(sessions, replicas, vnodes):
    return {s: replicas[len(s) % len(replicas)] for s in sessions} if replicas else {}
--- solution
import bisect
import hashlib


def h(s):
    return int.from_bytes(hashlib.sha256(s.encode()).digest()[:8], "big")


def ring_route(sessions, replicas, vnodes):
    if not replicas:
        return {}
    ring = sorted((h(f"{r}#{i}"), r) for r in replicas for i in range(vnodes))
    points = [p for p, _ in ring]
    out = {}
    for s in sessions:
        k = bisect.bisect_left(points, h(s))
        out[s] = ring[k % len(ring)][1]
    return out
--- hint
Build a sorted list of `(point, replica)` pairs for every virtual node. `bisect.bisect_left` on the list of points finds the first point at or after the session's hash.
--- hint
If the search lands past the end, wrap round to index 0: `ring[k % len(ring)]`.
--- check case | Every session is assigned, and the same session always lands in the same place
(lambda a, b: (len(a), a == b))(ring_route([f"chat-{i}" for i in range(50)], ["r0", "r1", "r2"], 40), ring_route([f"chat-{i}" for i in range(50)], ["r0", "r1", "r2"], 40))
=> (50, True)
--- check case | The first few assignments
(lambda d: [d[f"chat-{i}"] for i in range(6)])(ring_route([f"chat-{i}" for i in range(50)], ["r0", "r1", "r2"], 40))
=> ['r2', 'r2', 'r1', 'r2', 'r0', 'r0']
--- check test | Removing a replica moves only its own sessions
(lambda a, b: all(a[s] == b[s] for s in a if a[s] != "r1") and all(b[s] != "r1" for s in b))(ring_route([f"chat-{i}" for i in range(500)], ["r0", "r1", "r2", "r3"], 50), ring_route([f"chat-{i}" for i in range(500)], ["r0", "r2", "r3"], 50))
--- check test | With many virtual nodes, no replica gets more than twice its share
(lambda d: max(__import__("collections").Counter(d.values()).values()) < 2 * 1000 / 4)(ring_route([f"user-{i}" for i in range(1000)], ["a", "b", "c", "d"], 100))
--- check case | No replicas, and one replica
(ring_route(["x"], [], 10), ring_route(["x", "y"], ["only"], 3))
=> ({}, {"x": "only", "y": "only"})

+++ practice | Debug: the router that herds
--- task
`least_loaded(prompts, n)` should send each prompt to the replica with the least load so far (the total prompt tokens it has been sent; the smallest index on a tie) and return the list of choices. In a burst of requests every one of them lands on replica 0, which then queues while the others sit idle: head-of-line blocking caused by the router itself. For `least_loaded([[1] * 10, [2] * 10, [3] * 10], 3)` it returns `[0, 0, 0]` instead of `[0, 1, 2]`. Find the bug and fix it.
--- starter
def least_loaded(prompts, n):
    load = [0] * n
    choices = []
    for p in prompts:
        r = min(range(n), key=lambda i: (load[i], i))
        choices.append(r)
    return choices
--- solution
def least_loaded(prompts, n):
    load = [0] * n
    choices = []
    for p in prompts:
        r = min(range(n), key=lambda i: (load[i], i))
        choices.append(r)
        load[r] += len(p)
    return choices
--- hint
The router reads the loads to choose. Where does it ever change them?
--- hint
After choosing replica r, record the work just sent there: `load[r] += len(p)`. Without it, every load stays 0 and the tie always goes to replica 0.
--- check case | The burst from the task
least_loaded([[1] * 10, [2] * 10, [3] * 10], 3)
=> [0, 1, 2]
--- check case | A long prompt keeps its replica busy for a while
least_loaded([[1] * 100, [2] * 10, [3] * 10, [4] * 10, [5] * 10], 2)
=> [0, 1, 1, 1, 1]
--- check case | One replica takes everything
least_loaded([[1], [2, 2]], 1)
=> [0, 0]
--- check case | No prompts
least_loaded([], 4)
=> []

+++ practice | Cache-aware routing with a load guard
--- task
Put time into the routing problem. Prompt t arrives at `arrivals[t]` (seconds, in increasing order). A replica prefills only the uncached part of a prompt, at `rate` tokens per second, one prompt at a time in arrival order: a prompt sent to replica r starts at max(its arrival, the time r becomes free), waits from its arrival until that start, and keeps r busy for (uncached tokens) / `rate` seconds. A replica's **load** when a prompt arrives is how much work it still has queued, max(0, the time it becomes free − the arrival), in seconds.

Each replica has its own `RadixCache(capacity)` from the starter. Write `simulate(prompts, arrivals, n, capacity, rate, policy, slack)`. For each prompt, find each replica's match `m[r]` without refreshing times and its load, then pick a replica:

- `"least"`: the smallest load (smallest index on a tie);
- `"cache"`: the largest match (on a tie the smaller load, then the smaller index);
- `"guarded"`: the `"cache"` choice, unless its load is more than `slack` seconds above the smallest load; then the `"least"` choice.

On the chosen replica, call `match(prompt, t)` then `insert(prompt, t)`. Return `(hits, mean_wait)`: the total of the chosen matches, and the average wait rounded to 6 decimal places.
--- starter


class Node:
    def __init__(self, tokens, parent):
        self.tokens = tokens
        self.parent = parent
        self.children = {}
        self.last_used = 0


def shared(a, b):
    n = 0
    while n < len(a) and n < len(b) and a[n] == b[n]:
        n += 1
    return n


class RadixCache:
    """Cached token prefixes in a radix tree, at most `capacity` tokens, evicting least recently used leaves."""

    def __init__(self, capacity):
        self.capacity = capacity
        self.root = Node((), None)
        self.total = 0

    def size(self):
        return self.total

    def match(self, tokens, now=None):
        tokens = tuple(tokens)
        node, i = self.root, 0
        while i < len(tokens) and tokens[i] in node.children:
            child = node.children[tokens[i]]
            n = shared(child.tokens, tokens[i:])
            if now is not None:
                child.last_used = now
            i += n
            if n < len(child.tokens):
                break
            node = child
        return i

    def insert(self, tokens, now=0):
        tokens = tuple(tokens)
        node, i = self.root, 0
        while i < len(tokens):
            child = node.children.get(tokens[i])
            if child is None:
                leaf = Node(tokens[i:], node)
                leaf.last_used = now
                node.children[tokens[i]] = leaf
                self.total += len(tokens) - i
                break
            n = shared(child.tokens, tokens[i:])
            if n < len(child.tokens):
                mid = Node(child.tokens[:n], node)
                node.children[tokens[i]] = mid
                child.tokens = child.tokens[n:]
                child.parent = mid
                mid.children[child.tokens[0]] = child
                child = mid
            child.last_used = now
            node = child
            i += n
        self.evict()

    def path(self, node):
        parts = []
        while node is not self.root:
            parts.append(node.tokens)
            node = node.parent
        return tuple(t for part in reversed(parts) for t in part)

    def evict(self):
        while self.total > self.capacity:
            leaves, stack = [], [self.root]
            while stack:
                node = stack.pop()
                stack.extend(node.children.values())
                if not node.children and node is not self.root:
                    leaves.append(node)
            victim = min(leaves, key=lambda n: (n.last_used, self.path(n)))
            del victim.parent.children[victim.tokens[0]]
            self.total -= len(victim.tokens)


import random


def make_prompts(seed, n, n_system=4, system_len=200, user_len=(20, 80), vocab=1000, weights=None):
    """n chat prompts: one of n_system shared system prompts (chosen with the given weights,
    or uniformly), then the user's own tokens."""
    rng = random.Random(seed)
    systems = [[rng.randrange(vocab) for _ in range(system_len)] for _ in range(n_system)]
    out = []
    for _ in range(n):
        user = [rng.randrange(vocab) for _ in range(rng.randint(*user_len))]
        pick = rng.randrange(n_system) if weights is None else rng.choices(range(n_system), weights)[0]
        out.append(systems[pick] + user)
    return out


def simulate(prompts, arrivals, n, capacity, rate, policy, slack):
    return (0, 0.0)
--- solution


class Node:
    def __init__(self, tokens, parent):
        self.tokens = tokens
        self.parent = parent
        self.children = {}
        self.last_used = 0


def shared(a, b):
    n = 0
    while n < len(a) and n < len(b) and a[n] == b[n]:
        n += 1
    return n


class RadixCache:
    """Cached token prefixes in a radix tree, at most `capacity` tokens, evicting least recently used leaves."""

    def __init__(self, capacity):
        self.capacity = capacity
        self.root = Node((), None)
        self.total = 0

    def size(self):
        return self.total

    def match(self, tokens, now=None):
        tokens = tuple(tokens)
        node, i = self.root, 0
        while i < len(tokens) and tokens[i] in node.children:
            child = node.children[tokens[i]]
            n = shared(child.tokens, tokens[i:])
            if now is not None:
                child.last_used = now
            i += n
            if n < len(child.tokens):
                break
            node = child
        return i

    def insert(self, tokens, now=0):
        tokens = tuple(tokens)
        node, i = self.root, 0
        while i < len(tokens):
            child = node.children.get(tokens[i])
            if child is None:
                leaf = Node(tokens[i:], node)
                leaf.last_used = now
                node.children[tokens[i]] = leaf
                self.total += len(tokens) - i
                break
            n = shared(child.tokens, tokens[i:])
            if n < len(child.tokens):
                mid = Node(child.tokens[:n], node)
                node.children[tokens[i]] = mid
                child.tokens = child.tokens[n:]
                child.parent = mid
                mid.children[child.tokens[0]] = child
                child = mid
            child.last_used = now
            node = child
            i += n
        self.evict()

    def path(self, node):
        parts = []
        while node is not self.root:
            parts.append(node.tokens)
            node = node.parent
        return tuple(t for part in reversed(parts) for t in part)

    def evict(self):
        while self.total > self.capacity:
            leaves, stack = [], [self.root]
            while stack:
                node = stack.pop()
                stack.extend(node.children.values())
                if not node.children and node is not self.root:
                    leaves.append(node)
            victim = min(leaves, key=lambda n: (n.last_used, self.path(n)))
            del victim.parent.children[victim.tokens[0]]
            self.total -= len(victim.tokens)


import random


def make_prompts(seed, n, n_system=4, system_len=200, user_len=(20, 80), vocab=1000, weights=None):
    """n chat prompts: one of n_system shared system prompts (chosen with the given weights,
    or uniformly), then the user's own tokens."""
    rng = random.Random(seed)
    systems = [[rng.randrange(vocab) for _ in range(system_len)] for _ in range(n_system)]
    out = []
    for _ in range(n):
        user = [rng.randrange(vocab) for _ in range(rng.randint(*user_len))]
        pick = rng.randrange(n_system) if weights is None else rng.choices(range(n_system), weights)[0]
        out.append(systems[pick] + user)
    return out


def simulate(prompts, arrivals, n, capacity, rate, policy, slack):
    caches = [RadixCache(capacity) for _ in range(n)]
    free_at = [0.0] * n
    hits, waits = 0, []
    for t, (p, arrival) in enumerate(zip(prompts, arrivals)):
        m = [c.match(p) for c in caches]
        load = [max(0.0, f - arrival) for f in free_at]
        least = min(range(n), key=lambda i: (load[i], i))
        cache = min(range(n), key=lambda i: (-m[i], load[i], i))
        if policy == "least":
            r = least
        elif policy == "cache":
            r = cache
        else:
            r = cache if load[cache] - load[least] <= slack else least
        caches[r].match(p, t)
        caches[r].insert(p, t)
        start = max(arrival, free_at[r])
        waits.append(start - arrival)
        free_at[r] = start + (len(p) - m[r]) / rate
        hits += m[r]
    return (hits, round(sum(waits) / len(waits), 6))
--- hint
Keep, per replica, the time it becomes free. A prompt's load on replica r is `max(0.0, free_at[r] - arrival)`; after choosing r, its start is `max(arrival, free_at[r])` and `free_at[r]` moves on by the uncached tokens divided by `rate`.
--- hint
Compute both candidate choices every time: the least loaded, and the best match (key `(-m[i], load[i], i)`). The guarded policy takes the match unless its load exceeds the least load by more than `slack`.
--- check case | One hot system prompt among eight, small caches: all three policies
[simulate(make_prompts(4, 400, n_system=8, weights=[6, 1, 1, 1, 1, 1, 1, 1]), [i * 0.05 for i in range(400)], 4, 1000, 500.0, pol, 0.3) for pol in ("least", "cache", "guarded")]
=> [(46000, 4.1176), (77802, 0.202345), (73801, 0.086465)]
?? Least-loaded scatters each system prompt over every replica, whose small caches then keep evicting them: few hits, so much more prefill work and long waits.
--- check test | The guard keeps most of the hits and waits less than either pure policy
(lambda l, c, g: g[0] > 1.5 * l[0] and g[1] < c[1] and g[1] < l[1])(*[simulate(make_prompts(4, 400, n_system=8, weights=[6, 1, 1, 1, 1, 1, 1, 1]), [i * 0.05 for i in range(400)], 4, 1000, 500.0, pol, 0.3) for pol in ("least", "cache", "guarded")])
--- check case | Two prompts on two replicas
simulate([[1, 2, 3, 4], [1, 2, 3, 5]], [0.0, 0.0], 2, 100, 1.0, "cache", 0.0)
=> (3, 2.0)
?? The second prompt matches 3 tokens on replica 0, which is busy for 4 more seconds, but the cache policy goes there anyway: it waits 4 s, so the mean is 2.0.
--- check case | The guarded policy sends it elsewhere
simulate([[1, 2, 3, 4], [1, 2, 3, 5]], [0.0, 0.0], 2, 100, 1.0, "guarded", 1.0)
=> (0, 0.0)

=== mlsys2-07 | Autoscaling model servers: cold starts, warm pools and the right signal
--- teach
Lesson 3 found a replica's knee: past about 10 requests a second, goodput collapsed. Lesson 6 spread requests over many replicas. But traffic is never flat. It follows the day, doubles when a product launches, and spikes when a batch job starts. Run enough replicas for the peak all day and most of them idle at night, each one an accelerator that costs money every hour. **Autoscaling** changes the number of replicas to follow demand. For model servers it is harder than for web servers, for one reason: a new replica takes a long time to become useful.

### Why a model server starts slowly

A web server process starts in about a second. A model server replica goes through four phases before it serves its first token:

1. **Provisioning**: find a machine with a free accelerator and start it. If the cluster has one idle, seconds; if a new machine must be acquired from a cloud provider, minutes, and sometimes there is none to be had.
2. **Pulling the image**: the container image, with its libraries, is often several GB.
3. **Loading the weights**: 14 GB for the 7B model in 16-bit, 140 GB for a 70B one. From object storage at 1 GB/s, the 70B model takes over two minutes; from a local disk at 5 GB/s, about 28 s; from host memory over a fast link at 25 GB/s, under 6 s.
4. **Warming up**: compiling or tuning kernels for the shapes it will see, capturing execution graphs, and allocating the KV cache pool. Tens of seconds is common.

Add them up: a **[[cold start|cold-start]]** of a large model takes from about a minute to many minutes. During that time the replica costs money and serves nothing, and the traffic that triggered the scale-up is queueing.

### Warm pools

The standard fix is to do the slow phases ahead of time. A **warm pool** is a set of standby machines that are already provisioned, with the image pulled and the weights loaded into host memory (or even onto the accelerator), but not serving. Turning one into a replica takes seconds. When a pool machine is used, the pool starts a cold start in the background to replace it.

A warm pool costs money too, since standby machines are paid for, so its size is a trade-off between that cost and the queueing during a spike. This lesson's last practice problem prices it.

### The signal to scale on

An autoscaler needs a measurement that says "too few replicas". Picking it is where many autoscalers go wrong.

- **Accelerator utilization** is a poor signal. The usual "utilization" number reports the fraction of time any kernel is running, so a replica decoding one request at a time, memory-bound and with its compute 99% idle (lesson 1), reports nearly 100%. It cannot tell a replica with spare batch slots from a saturated one.
- **Requests per second** misleads because requests differ: one request with a 50,000-token prompt is more work than a hundred short ones.
- **Tokens per second** against a replica's measured capacity, **queue depth** (requests or tokens waiting), **KV cache usage**, and **TTFT** itself are the signals that track real saturation. Queue depth reacts fastest: lesson 3's table showed TTFT exploding exactly when the queue started to grow.

### Target tracking

The common policy is **target tracking**: keep a load metric at a target fraction of capacity. With demand D tokens per second, a capacity of C tokens per second per replica and a target utilization u (say 0.7, leaving headroom for bursts):

**desired replicas = ⌈D / (C · u)⌉**

A queue that has already built up must also be cleared. Adding the backlog B divided by the time you allow for draining it, T, gives

**desired replicas = ⌈(D + B / T) / (C · u)⌉**

Two refinements stop the autoscaler from fighting itself:

- **Count replicas that are starting.** A replica in its cold start is not serving yet, but it is coming. An autoscaler that compares the desired number with only the **ready** replicas starts more and more replicas on every tick of a cold start, and overshoots badly.
- **Scale up fast, scale down slowly.** Demand is noisy. Removing a replica the moment demand dips, then paying a cold start when it comes back a minute later, is called **flapping**. The usual guard is a **[[stabilization window|stabilization]]**: scale down only to the largest desired count seen over the last few minutes. Cold starts make this matter more for model servers than for anything else.

Policies like these react to load that has already arrived, so they always lag by at least one cold start; that is why many teams also [[scale ahead of forecast demand|forecast]].

### The simulated fleet

The lab simulates a fleet in discrete **ticks** (think of a tick as a few seconds). Each tick, work arrives as a number of tokens, ready replicas serve up to `capacity` tokens each, and whatever is left waits in the **backlog**. Then the autoscaler's **policy** looks at the state and returns the number of replicas it wants. New replicas come from the warm pool if a standby machine is free (ready after `warm` ticks, and the pool slot is refilled `cold` ticks later) or start cold (ready after `cold` ticks). The cost of a run is its **replica-ticks**: the number of replicas allocated, ready or starting, added up over all ticks.

Here is the picture, as numbers. Demand jumps from 500 to 3,000 tokens per tick for 30 ticks, capacity is 1,000 tokens per tick per replica, cold starts take 20 ticks and warm starts 2. With no warm pool, the backlog peaks at 40,000 tokens and the fleet overshoots to 12 replicas while it drains. With a warm pool of 2, the backlog peaks at 4,000 tokens, and the run costs about half the replica-ticks.

**Watch out:**

- **Count the replicas that are starting**, or every tick of a cold start adds more.
- **Utilization near 100% does not mean full.** A memory-bound replica looks busy at any load.
- **Do not scale down on one quiet tick.** Use a window, and remember that every replica removed may cost a cold start to get back.
- **Scale-to-zero** saves money on idle models, but the first request after it waits for a full cold start.

::: context cold-start Where the minutes go
The slowest phase depends on where the bytes come from. Container images can be streamed lazily, fetching only the files a process actually touches, which cuts pulls from minutes to seconds. Weights can be loaded straight from fast local disks or peer machines instead of remote object storage, and loaded in parallel across all the accelerators of a server. Some systems snapshot a fully warmed process, memory and all, and restore it, skipping the warm-up. Each technique attacks one line of the sum, which is why measuring the four phases separately is the first step.
:::

::: context stabilization Why scaling down waits
Container orchestrators expose this directly: a widely used autoscaler, for example, scales down only to the highest recommendation it computed over the previous five minutes by default, while scaling up immediately. The asymmetry reflects the costs. Scaling up late hurts users at once; scaling down late costs a few replica-minutes of money. For model servers, whose cold starts take minutes, teams often use even longer windows, or keep a minimum number of replicas warm around the daily peak.
:::

::: context forecast Scaling before the spike
Reactive autoscaling always lags by at least one cold start. Traffic to most services follows strong daily and weekly patterns, so many teams add **predictive** scaling: forecast the next hour's demand from the same hour on previous days and start replicas early. Scheduled events, such as a launch or a nightly batch job, are scaled for in advance by hand. Reactive scaling then only has to cover the forecast's errors.
:::
--- task
Write the fleet simulator and a target-tracking policy.

**`run_fleet(demand, capacity, cold, warm, pool, policy, initial=1)`**: `demand[t]` is the tokens arriving in tick t. The fleet starts with `initial` ready replicas, nothing starting, a backlog of 0 and `pool` free warm-pool machines. Each tick t, in this order:

1. Every starting replica whose ready tick is t or earlier becomes ready; every pool refill due at t or earlier adds 1 free pool machine.
2. Add `demand[t]` to the backlog, then serve: the backlog drops by the smaller of itself and ready × `capacity`.
3. Call `policy(state)` with a dict holding `"tick"`, `"demand"` (this tick's demand), `"backlog"` (after serving), `"ready"`, `"starting"` (how many are starting) and `"capacity"`. It returns the number of replicas wanted.
4. Let have = ready + starting. If wanted > have, start wanted − have replicas, one at a time: if a pool machine is free, take it (ready at tick t + `warm`) and schedule its refill at t + `cold`; otherwise start cold (ready at t + `cold`). If wanted < have, remove have − wanted replicas: starting ones first, latest ready tick first; then ready ones.
5. Append `(ready, starting, backlog)` to the log.

Return the log.

**`target_tracking(target, drain, down_window)`** returns a policy. Each call computes desired = ⌈(demand + backlog / `drain`) / (capacity × `target`)⌉, at least 1, remembers it, and returns the **largest** desired value among the last `down_window` calls (this one included).
--- starter
import math


def run_fleet(demand, capacity, cold, warm, pool, policy, initial=1):
    log = []
    backlog = 0
    for t, d in enumerate(demand):
        backlog += d
        backlog -= min(backlog, initial * capacity)
        log.append((initial, 0, backlog))
    return log


def target_tracking(target, drain, down_window):
    def policy(s):
        return s["ready"]

    return policy
--- solution
import math

def run_fleet(demand, capacity, cold, warm, pool, policy, initial=1):
    ready, starting, refills = initial, [], []
    free_pool, backlog, log = pool, 0, []
    for t, d in enumerate(demand):
        ready += sum(1 for s in starting if s <= t)
        starting = [s for s in starting if s > t]
        free_pool += sum(1 for s in refills if s <= t)
        refills = [s for s in refills if s > t]
        backlog += d
        backlog -= min(backlog, ready * capacity)
        want = policy({"tick": t, "demand": d, "backlog": backlog, "ready": ready,
                       "starting": len(starting), "capacity": capacity})
        have = ready + len(starting)
        for _ in range(want - have):
            if free_pool > 0:
                free_pool -= 1
                starting.append(t + warm)
                refills.append(t + cold)
            else:
                starting.append(t + cold)
        excess = have - want
        while excess > 0 and starting:
            starting.remove(max(starting))
            excess -= 1
        if excess > 0:
            ready -= excess
        log.append((ready, len(starting), backlog))
    return log


def target_tracking(target, drain, down_window):
    history = []

    def policy(s):
        need = (s["demand"] + s["backlog"] / drain) / (s["capacity"] * target)
        history.append(max(1, math.ceil(need)))
        return max(history[-down_window:])

    return policy


--- hint
Keep a list of the ready ticks of the starting replicas and a list of the refill ticks of used pool machines. At the top of each tick, count and drop the entries that are due.
--- hint
When scaling up, check the free pool count for each new replica. When scaling down, remove from the starting list with `starting.remove(max(starting))` while there is still excess, then subtract the rest from `ready`.
--- hint
`target_tracking` keeps a list of every desired value in its closure, appends `max(1, math.ceil(need))` each call, and returns `max(history[-down_window:])`.

=> [(1, 1, 1000), (1, 1, 2000), (1, 1, 3000), (2, 0, 3000), (2, 0, 3000), (2, 0, 3000)]

=> [(1, 1, 0), (2, 0, 0), (2, 1, 0), (2, 1, 0), (2, 1, 0), (2, 1, 0), (3, 0, 0), (3, 0, 0)]
?? The first new replica takes the only pool machine at tick 0, ready at tick 1. The pool refills only at tick 4, too late for the replica wanted at tick 2: it starts cold and is ready at tick 6.

=> [(2, 1, 0), (2, 0, 0), (1, 0, 0), (0, 0, 0)]

--- check test | The fleet never counts negative replicas and drains the backlog by the end
