+++ problem | Pinning prefixes in the radix cache
--- task
The starter has the lesson's `RadixCache`. A running request's prefix must never be evicted while it runs. Add pinning:

- `lock(tokens)` adds 1 to a lock count on every node the walk of `match(tokens)` enters (including a node where the match ends partway); `unlock(tokens)` subtracts 1 from the same nodes. Nodes start with a lock count of 0.
- Eviction removes only leaves whose lock count is 0, choosing among them exactly as before. If the tree is over capacity but no leaf is unlocked, eviction stops (it must not crash).
- When `insert` splits a node, the new shared part gets the same lock count as the node it was split from (the walk of every lock passed through both parts).
--- starter


class Node:
    def __init__(self, tokens, parent):
        self.tokens = tokens
        self.parent = parent
        self.children = {}
        self.last_used = 0


def shared(a, b):
    n = 0
    while n < len(a) and n < len(b) and a[n] == b[n]:
        n += 1
    return n


class RadixCache:
    """Cached token prefixes in a radix tree, at most `capacity` tokens, evicting least recently used leaves."""

    def __init__(self, capacity):
        self.capacity = capacity
        self.root = Node((), None)
        self.total = 0

    def size(self):
        return self.total

    def match(self, tokens, now=None):
        tokens = tuple(tokens)
        node, i = self.root, 0
        while i < len(tokens) and tokens[i] in node.children:
            child = node.children[tokens[i]]
            n = shared(child.tokens, tokens[i:])
            if now is not None:
                child.last_used = now
            i += n
            if n < len(child.tokens):
                break
            node = child
        return i

    def insert(self, tokens, now=0):
        tokens = tuple(tokens)
        node, i = self.root, 0
        while i < len(tokens):
            child = node.children.get(tokens[i])
            if child is None:
                leaf = Node(tokens[i:], node)
                leaf.last_used = now
                node.children[tokens[i]] = leaf
                self.total += len(tokens) - i
                break
            n = shared(child.tokens, tokens[i:])
            if n < len(child.tokens):
                mid = Node(child.tokens[:n], node)
                node.children[tokens[i]] = mid
                child.tokens = child.tokens[n:]
                child.parent = mid
                mid.children[child.tokens[0]] = child
                child = mid
            child.last_used = now
            node = child
            i += n
        self.evict()

    def path(self, node):
        parts = []
        while node is not self.root:
            parts.append(node.tokens)
            node = node.parent
        return tuple(t for part in reversed(parts) for t in part)

    def evict(self):
        while self.total > self.capacity:
            leaves, stack = [], [self.root]
            while stack:
                node = stack.pop()
                stack.extend(node.children.values())
                if not node.children and node is not self.root:
                    leaves.append(node)
            victim = min(leaves, key=lambda n: (n.last_used, self.path(n)))
            del victim.parent.children[victim.tokens[0]]
            self.total -= len(victim.tokens)


    def lock(self, tokens):
        pass

    def unlock(self, tokens):
        pass
--- solution
class Node:
    def __init__(self, tokens, parent):
        self.tokens = tokens
        self.parent = parent
        self.children = {}
        self.last_used = 0
        self.locks = 0


def shared(a, b):
    n = 0
    while n < len(a) and n < len(b) and a[n] == b[n]:
        n += 1
    return n


class RadixCache:
    def __init__(self, capacity):
        self.capacity = capacity
        self.root = Node((), None)
        self.total = 0

    def size(self):
        return self.total

    def walk(self, tokens):
        tokens = tuple(tokens)
        node, i, seen = self.root, 0, []
        while i < len(tokens) and tokens[i] in node.children:
            child = node.children[tokens[i]]
            n = shared(child.tokens, tokens[i:])
            seen.append(child)
            i += n
            if n < len(child.tokens):
                break
            node = child
        return i, seen

    def match(self, tokens, now=None):
        length, seen = self.walk(tokens)
        if now is not None:
            for node in seen:
                node.last_used = now
        return length

    def lock(self, tokens):
        for node in self.walk(tokens)[1]:
            node.locks += 1

    def unlock(self, tokens):
        for node in self.walk(tokens)[1]:
            node.locks -= 1

    def insert(self, tokens, now=0):
        tokens = tuple(tokens)
        node, i = self.root, 0
        while i < len(tokens):
            child = node.children.get(tokens[i])
            if child is None:
                leaf = Node(tokens[i:], node)
                leaf.last_used = now
                node.children[tokens[i]] = leaf
                self.total += len(tokens) - i
                break
            n = shared(child.tokens, tokens[i:])
            if n < len(child.tokens):
                mid = Node(child.tokens[:n], node)
                mid.locks = child.locks
                node.children[tokens[i]] = mid
                child.tokens = child.tokens[n:]
                child.parent = mid
                mid.children[child.tokens[0]] = child
                child = mid
            child.last_used = now
            node = child
            i += n
        self.evict()

    def path(self, node):
        parts = []
        while node is not self.root:
            parts.append(node.tokens)
            node = node.parent
        return tuple(t for part in reversed(parts) for t in part)

    def evict(self):
        while self.total > self.capacity:
            leaves, stack = [], [self.root]
            while stack:
                node = stack.pop()
                stack.extend(node.children.values())
                if not node.children and node is not self.root and node.locks == 0:
                    leaves.append(node)
            if not leaves:
                return
            victim = min(leaves, key=lambda n: (n.last_used, self.path(n)))
            del victim.parent.children[victim.tokens[0]]
            self.total -= len(victim.tokens)
--- check case | A locked prefix survives, an unlocked one goes

=> (3, 0, 2, 5)
--- check case | With every older leaf locked, the new prompt is the one evicted
(lambda c: (c.insert([1, 2, 3], now=1), c.lock([1, 2, 3]), c.insert([4, 5, 6], now=2), c.lock([4, 5, 6]), c.insert([7, 8], now=3), c.size(), c.match([1, 2, 3]), c.match([4, 5, 6]), c.match([7, 8]))[5:])(RadixCache(6))
=> (6, 3, 3, 0)
?? Inserting [7, 8] makes 8 tokens; the only unlocked leaf is [7, 8] itself, so it goes, and eviction must then stop without crashing.
--- check case | After unlocking, the next insert evicts again
(lambda c: (c.insert([1, 2, 3], now=1), c.lock([1, 2, 3]), c.insert([4, 5, 6], now=2), c.lock([4, 5, 6]), c.insert([7], now=3), c.unlock([1, 2, 3]), c.insert([8], now=4), c.match([1, 2, 3]), c.match([4, 5, 6]), c.size())[7:])(RadixCache(6))
=> (0, 3, 4)
--- check case | A split keeps the lock on both parts
(lambda c: (c.insert([1, 2, 3, 4], now=1), c.lock([1, 2, 3, 4]), c.insert([1, 2, 9], now=2), c.insert([5, 5, 5, 5], now=3), c.match([1, 2, 3, 4]), c.match([1, 2, 9]), c.unlock([1, 2, 3, 4]), c.insert([6, 6, 6, 6], now=4), c.match([1, 2, 3, 4]), c.size())[4:6] + (c.match([1, 2, 3, 4]), c.size()))(RadixCache(5))
=> (4, 2, 0, 4)
--- check case | Locking a prompt that is only partly cached locks what is there
(lambda c: (c.insert([1, 2], now=1), c.lock([1, 2, 3, 4]), c.insert([9, 9, 9, 9], now=2), c.match([1, 2]), c.size())[3:])(RadixCache(4))
=> (2, 2)

+++ problem | An autoscaler that does not flap
--- task
The starter has lesson 7's `run_fleet`. Write a policy with **hysteresis**, and a way to count flapping.

`hysteresis(high, low, patience)` returns a policy. Each call, let have = ready + starting and r = backlog / max(1, have).

- If r > `high`: answer have + 1, and reset the quiet count to 0.
- Else if r < `low`: add 1 to the quiet count; if it has reached `patience`, reset it to 0 and answer max(1, have − 1); otherwise answer have.
- Otherwise: reset the quiet count to 0 and answer have.

`flaps(log)`: for a log from `run_fleet`, take the fleet size ready + starting at each tick and count the **reversals**: the times the size moves in the opposite direction to its last change (ticks where it stays the same are ignored).
--- starter


def run_fleet(demand, capacity, cold, warm, pool, policy, initial=1):
    ready, starting, refills = initial, [], []
    free_pool, backlog, log = pool, 0, []
    for t, d in enumerate(demand):
        ready += sum(1 for s in starting if s <= t)
        starting = [s for s in starting if s > t]
        free_pool += sum(1 for s in refills if s <= t)
        refills = [s for s in refills if s > t]
        backlog += d
        backlog -= min(backlog, ready * capacity)
        want = policy({"tick": t, "demand": d, "backlog": backlog, "ready": ready,
                       "starting": len(starting), "capacity": capacity})
        have = ready + len(starting)
        for _ in range(want - have):
            if free_pool > 0:
                free_pool -= 1
                starting.append(t + warm)
                refills.append(t + cold)
            else:
                starting.append(t + cold)
        excess = have - want
        while excess > 0 and starting:
            starting.remove(max(starting))
            excess -= 1
        if excess > 0:
            ready -= excess
        log.append((ready, len(starting), backlog))
    return log


def hysteresis(high, low, patience):
    def policy(s):
        return s["ready"] + s["starting"]

    return policy


def flaps(log):
    return 0
--- solution


def run_fleet(demand, capacity, cold, warm, pool, policy, initial=1):
    ready, starting, refills = initial, [], []
    free_pool, backlog, log = pool, 0, []
    for t, d in enumerate(demand):
        ready += sum(1 for s in starting if s <= t)
        starting = [s for s in starting if s > t]
        free_pool += sum(1 for s in refills if s <= t)
        refills = [s for s in refills if s > t]
        backlog += d
        backlog -= min(backlog, ready * capacity)
        want = policy({"tick": t, "demand": d, "backlog": backlog, "ready": ready,
                       "starting": len(starting), "capacity": capacity})
        have = ready + len(starting)
        for _ in range(want - have):
            if free_pool > 0:
                free_pool -= 1
                starting.append(t + warm)
                refills.append(t + cold)
            else:
                starting.append(t + cold)
        excess = have - want
        while excess > 0 and starting:
            starting.remove(max(starting))
            excess -= 1
        if excess > 0:
            ready -= excess
        log.append((ready, len(starting), backlog))
    return log


def hysteresis(high, low, patience):
    quiet = [0]

    def policy(s):
        have = s["ready"] + s["starting"]
        r = s["backlog"] / max(1, have)
        if r > high:
            quiet[0] = 0
            return have + 1
        if r < low:
            quiet[0] += 1
            if quiet[0] >= patience:
                quiet[0] = 0
                return max(1, have - 1)
            return have
        quiet[0] = 0
        return have

    return policy


def flaps(log):
    sizes = [r + s for r, s, _ in log]
    last, count = 0, 0
    for a, b in zip(sizes, sizes[1:]):
        if b == a:
            continue
        step = 1 if b > a else -1
        if last and step != last:
            count += 1
        last = step
    return count
--- check case | flaps on hand-made logs
(flaps([(1, 0, 0), (2, 0, 0), (2, 0, 0), (3, 0, 0), (2, 0, 0), (2, 0, 0), (3, 0, 0)]), flaps([(1, 0, 0)]), flaps([(1, 0, 0), (1, 1, 0), (3, 0, 0)]))
=> (2, 0, 0)
--- check case | The policy's answers on a sequence of states
(lambda p: [p({"tick": t, "demand": 0, "backlog": b, "ready": 2, "starting": 0, "capacity": 1000}) for t, b in enumerate([5000, 0, 0, 0, 1000, 0])])(hysteresis(2000, 100, 2))
=> [3, 2, 1, 2, 2, 2]
--- check test | On noisy demand, patience cuts the flapping
(lambda demand: flaps(run_fleet(demand, 1000, 3, 1, 0, hysteresis(500, 400, 1))) > 2 * flaps(run_fleet(demand, 1000, 3, 1, 0, hysteresis(1500, 100, 4))))([2000 + (i * 7919 % 13) * 150 for i in range(200)])
--- check case | The flap counts for both settings
(lambda demand: (flaps(run_fleet(demand, 1000, 3, 1, 0, hysteresis(500, 400, 1))), flaps(run_fleet(demand, 1000, 3, 1, 0, hysteresis(1500, 100, 4)))))([2000 + (i * 7919 % 13) * 150 for i in range(200)])
=> (32, 15)

+++ problem | A sharded vector index
--- task
The starter has lesson 8's `IVF`, `dist2`, `exact_search` and the data generators. A big index is split into shards on different machines. Write `class Sharded(vectors, n_shards, nlist)`:

- vector i goes to shard i % `n_shards`; each shard is an `IVF(its vectors, nlist)` (with the default iters and seed) over its own vectors, in increasing global id, so shard s's local id j is global id j · `n_shards` + s;
- `search(q, k, nprobe)`: ask every shard for its own `k` nearest with that `nprobe`, translate their ids to global ids, and merge: return the `k` global ids with the smallest `dist2` to `q` (ties to the smaller global id). Also set `self.last_scanned` to the total vectors scanned by all shards.
--- starter


import math
import random


def make_vectors(seed, n, d, n_clusters=10, spread=1.0):
    """n unit vectors of dimension d, bunched around n_clusters random directions."""
    rng = random.Random(seed)
    centers = [[rng.random() * 2 - 1 for _ in range(d)] for _ in range(n_clusters)]
    out = []
    for _ in range(n):
        c = centers[rng.randrange(n_clusters)]
        v = [x + (rng.random() * 2 - 1) * spread for x in c]
        norm = math.sqrt(sum(x * x for x in v))
        out.append([x / norm for x in v])
    return out


def dist2(a, b):
    return sum((x - y) ** 2 for x, y in zip(a, b))


def exact_search(vectors, q, k):
    return sorted(range(len(vectors)), key=lambda i: (dist2(vectors[i], q), i))[:k]


def make_dataset(seed, n, n_queries, d, n_clusters=10, spread=1.0):
    """A database of n vectors and n_queries query vectors from the same clusters."""
    both = make_vectors(seed, n + n_queries, d, n_clusters, spread)
    return both[:n], both[n:]


def nearest(centroids, v):
    return min(range(len(centroids)), key=lambda c: (dist2(centroids[c], v), c))


def kmeans(vectors, nlist, iters, seed):
    rng = random.Random(seed)
    centroids = [list(vectors[i]) for i in rng.sample(range(len(vectors)), nlist)]
    for _ in range(iters):
        members = [[] for _ in range(nlist)]
        for v in vectors:
            members[nearest(centroids, v)].append(v)
        for c, group in enumerate(members):
            if group:
                centroids[c] = [sum(xs) / len(group) for xs in zip(*group)]
    return centroids


class IVF:
    def __init__(self, vectors, nlist, iters=5, seed=0):
        self.vectors = vectors
        self.centroids = kmeans(vectors, nlist, iters, seed)
        self.lists = [[] for _ in range(nlist)]
        for i, v in enumerate(vectors):
            self.lists[nearest(self.centroids, v)].append(i)
        self.last_scanned = 0

    def search(self, q, k, nprobe):
        order = sorted(range(len(self.centroids)), key=lambda c: (dist2(self.centroids[c], q), c))
        candidates = [i for c in order[:nprobe] for i in self.lists[c]]
        self.last_scanned = len(candidates)
        return sorted(candidates, key=lambda i: (dist2(self.vectors[i], q), i))[:k]


def recall(found, truth):
    return len(set(found) & set(truth)) / len(truth)


class Sharded:
    def __init__(self, vectors, n_shards, nlist):
        self.vectors = vectors
        self.last_scanned = 0

    def search(self, q, k, nprobe):
        self.last_scanned = len(self.vectors)
        return exact_search(self.vectors, q, k)[::-1]
--- solution


import math
import random


def make_vectors(seed, n, d, n_clusters=10, spread=1.0):
    """n unit vectors of dimension d, bunched around n_clusters random directions."""
    rng = random.Random(seed)
    centers = [[rng.random() * 2 - 1 for _ in range(d)] for _ in range(n_clusters)]
    out = []
    for _ in range(n):
        c = centers[rng.randrange(n_clusters)]
        v = [x + (rng.random() * 2 - 1) * spread for x in c]
        norm = math.sqrt(sum(x * x for x in v))
        out.append([x / norm for x in v])
    return out


def dist2(a, b):
    return sum((x - y) ** 2 for x, y in zip(a, b))


def exact_search(vectors, q, k):
    return sorted(range(len(vectors)), key=lambda i: (dist2(vectors[i], q), i))[:k]


def make_dataset(seed, n, n_queries, d, n_clusters=10, spread=1.0):
    """A database of n vectors and n_queries query vectors from the same clusters."""
    both = make_vectors(seed, n + n_queries, d, n_clusters, spread)
    return both[:n], both[n:]


def nearest(centroids, v):
    return min(range(len(centroids)), key=lambda c: (dist2(centroids[c], v), c))


def kmeans(vectors, nlist, iters, seed):
    rng = random.Random(seed)
    centroids = [list(vectors[i]) for i in rng.sample(range(len(vectors)), nlist)]
    for _ in range(iters):
        members = [[] for _ in range(nlist)]
        for v in vectors:
            members[nearest(centroids, v)].append(v)
        for c, group in enumerate(members):
            if group:
                centroids[c] = [sum(xs) / len(group) for xs in zip(*group)]
    return centroids


class IVF:
    def __init__(self, vectors, nlist, iters=5, seed=0):
        self.vectors = vectors
        self.centroids = kmeans(vectors, nlist, iters, seed)
        self.lists = [[] for _ in range(nlist)]
        for i, v in enumerate(vectors):
            self.lists[nearest(self.centroids, v)].append(i)
        self.last_scanned = 0

    def search(self, q, k, nprobe):
        order = sorted(range(len(self.centroids)), key=lambda c: (dist2(self.centroids[c], q), c))
        candidates = [i for c in order[:nprobe] for i in self.lists[c]]
        self.last_scanned = len(candidates)
        return sorted(candidates, key=lambda i: (dist2(self.vectors[i], q), i))[:k]


def recall(found, truth):
    return len(set(found) & set(truth)) / len(truth)


class Sharded:
    def __init__(self, vectors, n_shards, nlist):
        self.vectors = vectors
        self.n = n_shards
        self.shards = [IVF(vectors[s::n_shards], nlist) for s in range(n_shards)]
        self.last_scanned = 0

    def search(self, q, k, nprobe):
        found, scanned = [], 0
        for s, shard in enumerate(self.shards):
            found += [j * self.n + s for j in shard.search(q, k, nprobe)]
            scanned += shard.last_scanned
        self.last_scanned = scanned
        return sorted(found, key=lambda i: (dist2(self.vectors[i], q), i))[:k]
--- check test | Probing every list in every shard is exact search
(lambda vs, qs: (lambda ix: all(ix.search(q, 10, 8) == exact_search(vs, q, 10) for q in qs))(Sharded(vs, 3, 8)))(*make_dataset(11, 600, 15, 8))
--- check case | Recall and vectors scanned with one list per shard
(lambda vs, qs: (lambda ix: (round(sum(recall(ix.search(q, 10, 1), exact_search(vs, q, 10)) for q in qs) / len(qs), 3), sum((ix.search(q, 10, 1), ix.last_scanned)[1] for q in qs)))(Sharded(vs, 4, 8)))(*make_dataset(12, 800, 20, 8))
=> (0.73, 2141)
--- check test | Ids are global: every returned vector is the one at that position
(lambda vs, qs: (lambda ix: all(sorted(dist2(vs[i], q) for i in ix.search(q, 5, 2)) == [dist2(vs[i], q) for i in ix.search(q, 5, 2)] for q in qs))(Sharded(vs, 5, 4)))(*make_dataset(13, 500, 10, 8))
--- check case | One shard is the plain index
(lambda vs, qs: all(Sharded(vs, 1, 8).search(q, 7, 2) == IVF(vs, 8).search(q, 7, 2) for q in qs))(*make_dataset(14, 300, 10, 8))
=> True

+++ problem | When separate prefill and decode pools pay
--- task
The starter has lesson 1's cost model. Compare serving a request on one kind of accelerator with **disaggregated** serving: prefill on accelerator P, then the KV cache sent over the network to accelerator D for decode.

Write `disagg(model, hw_p, price_p, hw_d, price_d, prompt_len, output_len, batch, net_bw)`. Prices are dollars per hour, `net_bw` is bytes per second. As in lesson 9, a request uses t_prefill (the time of `[(prompt_len, 0)]`) plus `output_len` × t_step / `batch` of accelerator time, where t_step is the time of `[(1, prompt_len + output_len // 2)] * batch`, each measured on the accelerator that runs that phase. Return a dict, with costs in dollars per 1,000 requests rounded to 4 decimal places:

- `"only_p"`: both phases on P, at `price_p`;
- `"only_d"`: both phases on D, at `price_d`;
- `"split"`: prefill on P at `price_p` plus decode on D at `price_d` (the transfer occupies neither accelerator);
- `"transfer_ms"`: the time to send the prompt's KV cache, `kv_bytes_per_token(model)` × `prompt_len` / `net_bw`, in milliseconds, rounded to 3 decimal places.
--- starter


import math
import random


class Model:
    """The shapes of a decoder-only transformer that set what serving it costs."""

    def __init__(self, n_params, n_layers, d_model, n_kv_heads, head_dim, bytes_per_param=2, kv_bytes=2):
        self.n_params = n_params
        self.n_layers = n_layers
        self.d_model = d_model
        self.n_kv_heads = n_kv_heads
        self.head_dim = head_dim
        self.bytes_per_param = bytes_per_param
        self.kv_bytes = kv_bytes


class Accel:
    """One accelerator: peak FLOP/s, memory bandwidth in bytes/s, memory size in bytes."""

    def __init__(self, peak_flops, mem_bw, mem_bytes):
        self.peak_flops = peak_flops
        self.mem_bw = mem_bw
        self.mem_bytes = mem_bytes


def kv_bytes_per_token(model):
    return 2 * model.n_layers * model.n_kv_heads * model.head_dim * model.kv_bytes


def step_cost(model, hw, work):
    if not work:
        return {"flops": 0, "bytes": 0, "time": 0.0, "bound": "idle"}
    tokens = sum(n for n, _ in work)
    keys = sum(n * c + n * (n + 1) // 2 for n, c in work)
    flops = 2 * model.n_params * tokens + 4 * model.n_layers * model.d_model * keys
    kv = kv_bytes_per_token(model)
    moved = model.n_params * model.bytes_per_param + kv * sum(c + n for n, c in work)
    t_compute = flops / hw.peak_flops
    t_memory = moved / hw.mem_bw
    bound = "compute" if t_compute > t_memory else "memory"
    return {"flops": flops, "bytes": moved, "time": max(t_compute, t_memory), "bound": bound}


def disagg(model, hw_p, price_p, hw_d, price_d, prompt_len, output_len, batch, net_bw):
    return {"only_p": 0.0, "only_d": 0.0, "split": 0.0, "transfer_ms": 0.0}
--- solution


import math
import random


class Model:
    """The shapes of a decoder-only transformer that set what serving it costs."""

    def __init__(self, n_params, n_layers, d_model, n_kv_heads, head_dim, bytes_per_param=2, kv_bytes=2):
        self.n_params = n_params
        self.n_layers = n_layers
        self.d_model = d_model
        self.n_kv_heads = n_kv_heads
        self.head_dim = head_dim
        self.bytes_per_param = bytes_per_param
        self.kv_bytes = kv_bytes


class Accel:
    """One accelerator: peak FLOP/s, memory bandwidth in bytes/s, memory size in bytes."""

    def __init__(self, peak_flops, mem_bw, mem_bytes):
        self.peak_flops = peak_flops
        self.mem_bw = mem_bw
        self.mem_bytes = mem_bytes


def kv_bytes_per_token(model):
    return 2 * model.n_layers * model.n_kv_heads * model.head_dim * model.kv_bytes


def step_cost(model, hw, work):
    if not work:
        return {"flops": 0, "bytes": 0, "time": 0.0, "bound": "idle"}
    tokens = sum(n for n, _ in work)
    keys = sum(n * c + n * (n + 1) // 2 for n, c in work)
    flops = 2 * model.n_params * tokens + 4 * model.n_layers * model.d_model * keys
    kv = kv_bytes_per_token(model)
    moved = model.n_params * model.bytes_per_param + kv * sum(c + n for n, c in work)
    t_compute = flops / hw.peak_flops
    t_memory = moved / hw.mem_bw
    bound = "compute" if t_compute > t_memory else "memory"
    return {"flops": flops, "bytes": moved, "time": max(t_compute, t_memory), "bound": bound}


def phases(model, hw, prompt_len, output_len, batch):
    t_prefill = step_cost(model, hw, [(prompt_len, 0)])["time"]
    t_step = step_cost(model, hw, [(1, prompt_len + output_len // 2)] * batch)["time"]
    return t_prefill, output_len * t_step / batch


def disagg(model, hw_p, price_p, hw_d, price_d, prompt_len, output_len, batch, net_bw):
    pre_p, dec_p = phases(model, hw_p, prompt_len, output_len, batch)
    pre_d, dec_d = phases(model, hw_d, prompt_len, output_len, batch)
    per_s_p, per_s_d = price_p / 3600, price_d / 3600
    return {
        "only_p": round((pre_p + dec_p) * per_s_p * 1000, 4),
        "only_d": round((pre_d + dec_d) * per_s_d * 1000, 4),
        "split": round((pre_p * per_s_p + dec_d * per_s_d) * 1000, 4),
        "transfer_ms": round(kv_bytes_per_token(model) * prompt_len / net_bw * 1000, 3),
    }
--- check case | Long prompts, compute-rich P and bandwidth-rich D
disagg(Model(7_000_000_000, 32, 4096, 8, 128), Accel(2e15, 2e12, 80e9), 4.0, Accel(5e14, 8e12, 141e9), 4.5, 6000, 400, 32, 50e9)
=> {'only_p': 0.3297, 'only_d': 0.3117, 'split': 0.1301, 'transfer_ms': 15.729}
--- check test | Splitting is never dearer than the better of the two phases on each machine
(lambda r: r["split"] <= min(r["only_p"], r["only_d"]) + 1e-4)(disagg(Model(7_000_000_000, 32, 4096, 8, 128), Accel(2e15, 2e12, 80e9), 4.0, Accel(5e14, 8e12, 141e9), 4.5, 6000, 400, 32, 50e9))
--- check case | Identical machines: splitting changes nothing
(lambda r: r["split"] == r["only_p"] == r["only_d"])(disagg(Model(7_000_000_000, 32, 4096, 8, 128), Accel(1e15, 3.35e12, 80e9), 4.0, Accel(1e15, 3.35e12, 80e9), 4.0, 1000, 300, 64, 50e9))
=> True
--- check case | The KV transfer of a 1,000-token prompt on a 25 GB/s link
disagg(Model(7_000_000_000, 32, 4096, 8, 128), Accel(1e15, 3.35e12, 80e9), 4.0, Accel(1e15, 3.35e12, 80e9), 4.0, 1000, 300, 64, 25e9)["transfer_ms"]
=> 5.243

+++ question | What the loop prints
--- ask
What does this print?

```python
layers, kv_heads, head_dim, nbytes = 16, 4, 64, 2
per_token = 2 * layers * kv_heads * head_dim * nbytes
print(per_token * 1000 // 2**20)
```
--- answer
15
--- why
2 × 16 × 4 × 64 × 2 = 16,384 bytes per token, 16 KiB. A thousand tokens take 16,384,000 bytes, and 16,384,000 // 1,048,576 = 15 MiB (15.6, rounded down by the floor division).

+++ question | Doubling the context
--- ask
A server decodes a batch of 128 requests at 8,000 tokens of context each, with the lesson-1 7B model, and the KV cache reads already exceed the weight reads. The contexts double to 16,000 tokens (and still fit). What happens to the step time, roughly?
--- choice
It stays about the same, because the weights are still read once per step.
--- choice correct
It nearly doubles, because the step is dominated by reading the KV cache, which doubles.
--- choice
It quadruples, because attention is quadratic in the context.
--- choice
It halves, because the batch now carries more tokens per step.
--- why
Each decode step reads every cached token of every request once: linear in the context, not quadratic (the quadratic cost is over a whole generation, not one step). When those reads dominate the step's bytes, doubling them nearly doubles the time.

+++ question | The copy that should not happen
--- ask
A paged allocator's `append` does copy-on-write like this: before writing, if the sequence's last block has a reference count above 1, it copies that block. Which case does this get wrong?
--- choice
A sequence whose last block is not shared.
--- choice correct
A shared last block that is completely full: the new token goes into a fresh block anyway, so the copy is wasted memory and time.
--- choice
A sequence with no blocks at all.
--- choice
A fork of a fork, where the count is 3.
--- why
Copy-on-write is needed only when a write would land inside a shared block, which happens only when that block is partly filled. A full shared block is never written again; the new token starts a new block, and the full block can stay shared for good.

+++ question | Why iteration-level scheduling wins
--- ask
Why does continuous batching give much lower TTFT than static batching at the same arrival rate?
--- choice
Because it prefills prompts faster.
--- choice
Because it uses a smaller batch.
--- choice correct
Because a new request joins at the next step after any slot frees, instead of waiting for the whole current batch, including its longest answer, to finish.
--- choice
Because it skips the prefill of repeated prompts.
--- why
Static batching holds slots for finished requests and blocks newcomers until the longest member ends. Continuous batching reschedules every step, so queueing time shrinks to roughly the time until the next request finishes.

+++ question | A perfect draft
--- ask
In speculative decoding with k = 5, the draft model happens to be identical to the target model. How many tokens does every step produce? Type a whole number.
--- answer
6
--- why
Every guess has p(x) = q(x), so it is accepted with probability min(1, p/q) = 1. All 5 guesses are kept, and the bonus token is drawn from the target after them: k + 1 = 6.

+++ question | Bits per weight
--- ask
Weights are stored in int4 with one 16-bit scale per group of 32. How many bits does each weight cost on average? Type a decimal number.
--- answer
4.5
4.5 bits
--- why
4 bits for the integer plus 16 / 32 = 0.5 bits for its share of the scale.

+++ question | The guarded router
--- ask
A cache-aware router sends each request to the replica with the longest cached prefix, unless that replica's load is more than a margin above the least-loaded one. What problem does the margin solve?
--- choice
It stops the radix trees from growing past their capacity.
--- choice correct
A popular prefix would otherwise pull most traffic onto one replica, which queues while others idle; the margin keeps most cache hits but caps that imbalance.
--- choice
It makes routing deterministic.
--- choice
It ensures every replica caches every prefix.
--- why
Pure cache affinity concentrates load on whichever replica cached a hot prefix first. Pure least-loaded scatters prefixes and loses hits. The margin follows the cache while the cost in queueing stays bounded.

+++ question | Loading the weights
--- ask
A 70B model in 16-bit (140 GB) must be loaded from object storage at 1 GB per second during a cold start. How many seconds does that phase take? Type a whole number.
--- answer
140
140 s
--- why
140 GB / 1 GB/s = 140 s, more than two minutes before warm-up even starts. That is why warm pools keep weights in host memory, where the same load takes a few seconds.

+++ question | Probing every list
--- ask
An IVF index has nlist = 64. What happens when a query is searched with nprobe = 64?
--- choice
The search fails, because nprobe must be smaller than nlist.
--- choice correct
It returns the exact nearest neighbours, but scans every vector plus all 64 centroids, so it is slightly slower than a plain exact search.
--- choice
Recall stays below 1, because k-means misassigns some vectors.
--- choice
It scans only the 64 centroids, so it is very fast.
--- why
Probing all lists makes every vector a candidate, and candidates are compared exactly, so the result is exact. The centroid comparisons are pure overhead in that case.

+++ question | Quarter-full fleet
--- ask
A fleet's average utilization falls from 1.0 to 0.25, with the same hardware, price and throughput when busy. By what factor does the cost per million tokens change? Type a whole number.
--- answer
4
4x
4 times
--- why
Cost per million tokens is price × 10⁶ / (3,600 × T × u). With u four times smaller, the cost is four times larger: the idle three quarters of the fleet are still paid for.
